# SECURITY.md — ASVS Level 2 Traceability Matrix

> **Status:** Skeleton, created 23/07/2026 per `AMENDMENTS-2026-07-17.md`
> §A2 / §A10 item 4. This file is the single security ledger of the Auto-MB
> SaaS: every OWASP **ASVS 5.0 Level 2** control in scope maps to *where it
> is implemented* and *which test proves it*. It doubles as the auditor
> handover pack for the STQC / CERT-In empanelled security audit.
>
> **Governance rule (from CLAUDE.md):** any PR that adds an endpoint, a
> module, an upload path, a queue consumer, or touches `packages/kernel`
> MUST update this matrix in the same PR. The gates listed in §2 may never
> be disabled, skipped, or downgraded. **They now block a merge.**
> `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs `pnpm gates` on every pull request and on
> pushes to `main` (OPS-15, `decisions/APPROVED-080` — PR runs cover every
> PR-branch push, and the required PR checks below are the wall),
> and as of 28/07/2026 **both** CI jobs — `frozen-effect` and `gates` — are
> *required* status checks on `main`. Both were measured, not read off a
> settings page (§2.1, §2.2): a pull request whose `gates` run failed while
> `frozen-effect` stayed green reported `mergeable_state: blocked`, where the
> same shape had reported `unstable` before the ruleset change. So §2.1's chain
> is genuinely build-failing in the sense an auditor means, for the first time
> since this file was written. §2 still splits into what `pnpm gates` enforces
> (§2.1) and what is named but does not exist (§2.2) — the latter is unchanged
> and remains owed. As of 28/07/2026 `scripts/tickets-check.sh` **and its
> negative probe are in the chain** (§2.1), so an invalid ticket now fails
> `gates` and blocks a merge. One limit stays: **no pull-request review is
> required** on `main` — direct pushes are refused, but an unreviewed PR
> merges once the required checks are green.
>
> **Scope note:** rows are populated for the **DC-first dogfooding
> milestone** (auth, tenancy, works, delivery challans, uploads, audit).
> MB / GST / billing controls enter the matrix when those phases are
> scoped, per ROADMAP. Rows marked `TBD` are owed before the stage that
> ships the feature exits.

---

## 0. Conventions

| Column | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **ASVS** | Control ID from the ASVS 5.0 checklist (populate exact IDs from the official checklist during Stage 0 — do not cite from memory) |
| **Requirement** | One-line paraphrase of the control |
| **Implementation** | File/module/mechanism that satisfies it |
| **Proving test** | The automated test (or CI gate) that fails if the control regresses |
| **Status** | `DONE` · `PARTIAL` · `TBD` · `N/A (reason)` |

Statuses are claims to an auditor — never mark `DONE` without a named
proving test. `N/A` rows must state why (e.g. "no OAuth flows in scope").

> ASVS column mapped against OWASP ASVS v5.0.0 (tag v5.0.0, May 2025). **Spot-check discharged 27/07/2026:** every distinct ID cited in this file (47 as of this edit) was checked against the official v5.0.0 flat JSON (`OWASP_Application_Security_Verification_Standard_5.0.0_en.flat.json`) and all resolve to real v5.0.0 requirements; the V3 (Web Frontend Security), V12 (Secure Communication) and V14 (Data Protection) chapter mappings are confirmed against that raw text, not a summary. **No V4 IDs are cited anywhere in this file** — §9's heading is a topic label; its rows cite V2/V15/V1/V16/V6 controls. Only one cited control is above the L2 bar (8.3.2, L3) and it is labelled as such in §4.
>
> **Chapter completeness (all 17 v5.0.0 chapters accounted for).** §3–§12 cover V1, V2, V3, V4, V5, V6, V7, V8, V11, V12, V13, V14, V15 and V16. The three remaining chapters carry no rows because they are **N/A to this architecture**, stated here rather than silently omitted: **V9 (Self-contained Tokens)** — sessions are server-side (§4/V7), the product mints no self-contained bearer token (JWT/PASETO) whose claims a client could forge or replay, so V9's token-integrity controls have no surface; **V10 (OAuth and OIDC)** — no OAuth/OIDC flows in scope (the DC-first milestone authenticates against the product's own credential store, no third-party IdP, authorization-code, or token-exchange path); **V17 (WebRTC)** — no WebRTC, real-time media, or peer-to-peer data channels anywhere in the product. Each becomes live only if the named capability is added, at which point its chapter earns a section here.

---

## 1. Architecture: the security kernel

All security primitives are consumed **only** via the public interfaces of
`packages/kernel` (import-boundary lint, build-failing). The kernel is the
audit-stable boundary: changes inside it classify as **full re-audit** in
the change-classification matrix (see §12, parked detail in
`docs/archive/2026-07-14-azure-adr/ARCHIVED-ARCHITECTURE.md` §12).

Kernel surface (Stage 0): authn/session adapter (Better Auth), authorizer
(two-tier cache + invalidation), tenant context / RLS session binding,
idempotency-key interceptor, audit writer, upload validation,
`assignNumber(tx, scope)`, crypto envelope helpers.

---

## 2. Security gates

> **Correction, 27/07/2026.** The previous version of this section listed
> eight gates as *"CI security gates (build-failing, live from Stage 0)"*.
> That was an overclaim on two independent counts, both re-measured for this
> edit rather than inferred:
>
> 1. **The CI runner arrived after this correction was written.**
>    `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (48 lines) now runs `pnpm gates` on GitHub's
>    infrastructure for every push and pull request. It is still not
>    build-failing in the sense an auditor means, because the `gates` check
>    is not a *required* status check — it reports, it does not block a
>    merge. See the CI-runner row in §2.2 for the outstanding owner action.
> 2. **Four of the eight named tools are absent**, and two more name
>    ESLint rules that do not exist in `eslint.config.js`.
>
> As of PR #3 (`059771d`) `origin/main` carries `package.json`,
> `eslint.config.js` and `.secretlintrc.json`, so §2.1's gates are on the
> default branch, not only on a feature branch. This correction records that
> the original claim was inflated, not that coverage was removed.

### 2.1 Wired today — the `pnpm gates` chain (verified green 27/07/2026)

| Gate | Tool | Proves | Invoked by |
|---|---|---|---|
| Lint | ESLint 9 (`js.configs.recommended` + `typescript-eslint` recommended) | Baseline JS/TS correctness. Since [SEC-4] the config also carries the kernel-boundary and frontend-ban rules — their own rows below | `pnpm lint` |
| Kernel boundary (import half) | ESLint `no-restricted-imports` in `eslint.config.js` ([SEC-4], `decisions/APPROVED-030`) | Everywhere outside `packages/kernel/**`, in **static `import`/`export` declarations**: no deep import past the kernel public entry (`@auto-mb/kernel/*` subpaths), no relative import resolving into `packages/kernel/`, no cloud SDKs (`@aws-sdk/*`, bare `aws-sdk`, `@azure/*` — ARCHITECTURE.md §10 discipline 3). `packages/kernel/**` itself is exempt (it may import its own internals and holds the SDKs). **Widened 29/07/2026 [SEC-7]** (`decisions/APPROVED-037` Option A, closing F6; round 2 after the cross-model review): the dynamic spellings are banned too — `import()` / `await import()` and `require()` of the same three target sets, where coverage is the full spelling product: require callee ∈ {bare `require`, `X.require`, `X["require"]`, `` X[`require`] ``} × argument ∈ {string literal, expression-free template literal} — the review measured `module.require`/`globalThis.require` and an aliased `require` slipping past the bare-callee-only first cut with plain literal arguments, inside the claimed class. **Aliasing `require` itself (`const r = require`) is now banned at the alias's creation** — receiver-scoped exactly as the document-alias cut is (round 3): the member arms are limited to the loader globals (`module`/`globalThis`/`global`/`self`/`window`), so a domain `.require` member — `pkg.exports["."]["require"]` is a standard package.json exports condition this workspace's own tests read — stays lawful, probe-asserted. `require.call`/`apply`/`bind` indirection is banned wholesale (round 3; no lawful product-code use). The boundary block's extension list gained `jsx`/`mts`/`cts` (a `.mts` product file was invisible to every block; `.jsx` had the sink bans but not the boundary — both review-measured). Each class carries a failing-fixture probe case asserting the selector's **own message**, not just the shared ruleId (the review deleted a whole selector family and the ruleId-only probe stayed green off a sibling); a dynamic import of the public entry `@auto-mb/kernel` and a domain-object field are probe-asserted quiet. Honest residues, stated not glossed: an `import()`/`require()` whose argument is a runtime-built expression, a loader produced by `createRequire()`, and a `require` alias obtained by **destructuring** (`const { require: r } = globalThis`) are undecidable by these selectors and stay uncovered by lint — the DC-5 source scan and review law carry those classes. Complements DC-1's package.json-level workspace-layout test and the source scan owed to DC-5: lint is the fast in-editor signal, the tests are the proof. Proven discriminating by `scripts/probe-eslint-gates.mjs` (`pnpm eslint-gates:probe`) | `pnpm lint`, `pnpm eslint-gates:probe` |
| Frontend bans | ESLint `no-eval` + `no-restricted-syntax` in `eslint.config.js` ([SEC-4], `decisions/APPROVED-030`) | In `packages/**` (product code): `eval` banned; the `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` JSX attribute, **direct** `.innerHTML`/`.outerHTML` assignment (`el.innerHTML = …`) and **literal** `document.write`/`writeln` calls banned as string-built-HTML sinks (§8 row `1.2.1, 1.3.2`). **Widened 29/07/2026 [SEC-7]** (`decisions/APPROVED-037` Option A, closing F8; round 2 after the cross-model review): computed members and `insertAdjacentHTML` are banned across the full key-spelling product {`.name`, `["literal"]`, `` [`template`] ``} — the review measured template-literal keys (`` el[`innerHTML`] ``) bypassing the literal-only first cut, inside the claimed class. The `document.write`/`writeln` **call** receiver set is deliberately broad (round 3): bare `document` plus **any** `.document` member in all three key spellings — `window["document"].write` defeated the round-1 cut, and round 2's global-qualified narrowing lost `frame.contentWindow.document.write()` (a textbook XSS sink with no lawful shape), both review-measured and both probe-cased now. The aliased-sink class (`const d = document; d.write(…)`, `const d = window["document"]`) is banned at the alias's **creation** — AST selectors cannot track data flow, so banning the aliasing of the GLOBAL document is the sound selector-level cut; the ban is deliberately scoped off `window`/`globalThis`/`self` so a **domain `document` field** (PRODUCT-SPEC PBG "amount, submitted amount, expiry, document" — `dto.document = row.document`) stays lawful, probe-asserted, and a `.write()` on a non-document receiver stays quiet (streams/responses do not trip it). **Corrected and closed 02/08/2026 [SEC-14]** (`decisions/APPROVED-073`, re-audit 2026-08-02 H3/H4/MED): the [SEC-7] claim that enabling `no-new-func` gave `new Function` a lint layer was **measured false for every spelling but the bare literal** — `no-new-func` is scope-based, so `const F = Function; F(src)` and `globalThis.Function(src)` evaded it AND the semgrep rule (which matches literal `new Function` only), leaving CWE-95 string-built code execution with zero automated cover for those spellings. The Function constructor is now selector-banned under the SEC-7 alias-at-creation doctrine, **hardened by the in-PR cross-model review (opus, REQUEST-CHANGES → all findings fixed and fixture-pinned in the same PR)**: alias **creation** from the bare global or a `globalThis`/`global`/`self`/`window` member in all three key spellings AND through **optional chaining** (`globalThis?.Function` — the review measured the `ChainExpression` wrapper defeating every plain member arm, review F2), plus the other decidable binding positions (`{F: Function}`, default parameters, class fields — review F7); **call/new on any receiver** (the SEC-7 round-3 broad-receiver rule applied symmetrically: `frame.contentWindow.Function(src)` is the constructor from another realm with no lawful shape **in this workspace's product code** — accepted FPs from the breadth are recorded below with their rewrites; only the ALIAS arms stay global-scoped, so a domain `.Function` field read stays lawful, probe control; review F3); and **indirect invocation** — `Function.call/apply/bind` bare and member-spelled, `Reflect.construct/apply(Function, …)` — mirroring the SEC-7 `require.call` arm (review F4). Closed with it: `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` via a **JSX spread**, descendant-matched so a conditional/nested/cast-wrapped source object literal is still seen, key spellings covering identifier, string literal AND expression-free template literal (reviews F1/F9); and the reflective HTML writes with honest sink semantics (review F5, measured against a prototype-setter harness): **`Reflect.set(el,"innerHTML",h)` invokes the setter** — the true assignment twin; **`{Object,Reflect}.defineProperty` is a *shadowing* write** ([[DefineOwnProperty]] does not consult the prototype chain, so it parses no HTML) banned as defense-in-depth, not claimed to be the sink; and **`Object.assign(el, {innerHTML: h})` — setter-invoking, the idiomatic spelling — is banned too**. Sink arms cover bare + one-level-qualified receivers (`globalThis.Reflect.set`), the three method-key spellings, and literal/template/TS-cast property arguments (`"innerHTML" as string`). Each spelling class carries a red-driving probe fixture asserting its own message, plus quiet controls (domain `.Function` member and `Function` type annotation, benign `Reflect.set`/`defineProperty`/`Object.assign` keys, the CJS `__esModule` idiom, variable and benign JSX spreads, docs/ exemption). Extension list gained `jsx`/`mts`/`cts` (review-measured invisibility). Each widened class carries a failing-fixture probe case asserting the selector's own message. Honest residues, stated not glossed: **aliasing** a document reached through a non-global receiver (`const d = frame.contentWindow.document; d.write(…)` — direct calls on such receivers ARE caught) and a sink keyed by a **runtime-computed expression** (`el[k] = …`) are undecidable at lint — review law and the semgrep layer carry what they can of those. The [SEC-14] classes add their own, same doctrine: a **variable** JSX spread (`{...props}` — data flow) and a variable `Object.assign` second argument, a runtime-computed key or property-name argument (`Reflect.set(el, k, h)`), `Object.defineProperties(el, {innerHTML: …})` (second-arg object walk, not a literal-arg call shape), a **destructured** `Function` (`const {Function: F} = globalThis`) and array-element references (`[Function]`) — the same disclosed destructuring residue the require arms carry — receivers qualified deeper than one member hop (`a.b.Reflect.set`), and the tokenless `(() => {}).constructor(src)` route stay undecidable-or-unbanned at lint and are carried by review law. A `SequenceExpression` alias init (`const F = (0, globalThis.Function)`) is the same wrapper class one node over, disclosed not banned (round-2 G1 N7). Review-measured accepted FPs, recorded with rewrites (the semgrep row's documented-FP discipline): a local variable shadowing a global name (`const window = {Function: "x"}; window.Function`) trips the alias ban — esquery is not scope-aware, identical to the pre-existing document/require arms; **`new lambda.Function(...)`** (the AWS CDK canonical spelling, incl. inside `packages/kernel/**` where ARCHITECTURE.md §10 puts cloud SDKs) trips the broad-receiver new arm — no CDK dependency exists today; if IaC ever lands, rename the import or move construct definitions out of the linted product tree; and a **type/validator registry object literal** (`{String, Number, Function}`) trips the binding-position alias ban — rewrite as `typeof x === "function"` checks or string tags. Owed follow-up (filed to `memory/inbox/`): the SEC-7 `document`-alias arms have the same optional-chain hole the review found here (`const d = window?.document` evades) — a SEC-7-scoped fix, not silently annexed to this ticket. `docs/` (the CEO dashboard) is deliberately out of scope — its `innerHTML` inventory is §12A's, and the probe asserts the exemption | `pnpm lint`, `pnpm eslint-gates:probe` |
| Typecheck | `tsc --noEmit` | Type errors | `pnpm typecheck` |
| Unit / integration | Vitest | **Product suites exist as of 04/08/2026** ([DC-2] `ab8a186`, [DC-22] `5072786`, [DC-3] `5d45235`) — the "none exist yet" claim this cell carried since 29/07/2026 is retired. **This cell deliberately no longer carries a per-file test inventory.** It carried one for a single day: the enumeration written on 04/08/2026 was stale by that evening (70 → 80 tests, one file 21 → 31) and the figure it replaced had been stale since OPS-18 `6a0836c` added workspace-layout case (h). A count no gate checks and every ticket invalidates reads to an auditor as a measurement while behaving as decoration, so what this cell records instead is the **collection rule and its blind spot**, which a reader can re-derive at any commit: root `vitest.config.ts` collects `packages/**/*.{test,spec}.{js,ts}` and nothing else — so (i) any package's plain unit/integration suite joins the chain the moment the file is added, with no registration step to forget, and (ii) **`*.dbtest.ts` files are structurally invisible to this gate**, their suffix not being `.test`/`.spec`. Dated snapshot, not a maintained figure — re-measured 05/08/2026 after wave 6 ([DC-24] `713a7e7`, [DC-26] `6efc465`): **11 files / 260 tests** in-chain, against **13 files / 254** in `packages/db`, **5 / 178** in `packages/loa` and **3 / 38** in `packages/works`. **The durable rule keeps being exercised in both directions, which is why the snapshot is kept and the per-file inventory is not:** `packages/loa` is now the largest suite in the repo and **every one of its 178 tests is in-chain** — it has no `.dbtest.ts` at all, because a pure library needs no cluster — while `packages/db` and `packages/works` keep adding tests the glob never sees. **Eleven `.dbtest.ts` files across two packages hold 218 tests no gate runs** (200 of the db package's 254, plus 18 of works' 38); the chain sees 260, and the arithmetic closes: 8 config + 54 db-plain + 178 loa + 20 works-plain = 260. The old bar — *no §3–§12 row may cite it until product code exists* — is released only in form: a row cites a **named test file**, never this aggregate step, and what the chain covers is the pure/unit half of `packages/db` plus `packages/loa`'s manifest/purity suite, not product behaviour behind an endpoint. **Stated bound — the DB-backed suites are NOT in this chain, and they now carry the load-bearing proofs:** `packages/db/test/*.dbtest.ts` holds the live-cluster proofs of the runner's out-of-order / checksum / transaction-mode refusals, the CVE-2025-8713 version floor, [DC-3]'s catalog-driven schema conventions (`schema-conventions.dbtest.ts` — itself DC-3's `verify:` line, so that ticket's acceptance evidence is *not* gate-run) the UTC partition-bound regression (`audit-log-partition-tz.dbtest.ts`), and — since `65c2a24` — [DC-4]'s catalog-driven RLS coverage (`rls-coverage.dbtest.ts`, 21 tests, the sole proof that tenant isolation is enabled at all) and [DC-45]'s units master (`units-master.dbtest.ts`, 57). Both are their tickets' `verify:` lines, so acceptance evidence keeps landing outside the merge gate — six tickets running as of wave 5, which now adds [DC-15]'s permanent work-code uniqueness (`work-code-uniqueness.dbtest.ts`, including the partial-index-would-be-wrong demonstration) and its service suite in a SECOND package (`packages/works/test/work-code-service.dbtest.ts`). None is matched by the root `include` glob; they run only under `pnpm --filter @auto-mb/db test` / `test:db` and now `pnpm --filter @auto-mb/works test` / `test:db`, which no gate invokes. **Second-order cost, recorded where the harness bound lives ([DC-15]):** because the DC-2 cluster harness is `packages/db`'s own test-only helper and `workspace-layout.test.ts` case (e) bans cross-package subpath imports, `packages/works` carries a 302-line COPY of `pg-cluster.ts` rather than importing it — a deliberate, documented rule-(e) casualty (its header says so and names the amendment that would retire it), not drift. It is a duplicate today and a divergence risk tomorrow: a fix to one harness does not reach the other, and no gate compares them. Any future row citing a `.dbtest.ts` file is citing a test **`pnpm gates` never runs** and must say so in its Proving-test cell. **Stated bound — this gate can go vacuously green (audit M-13):** root `vitest.config.ts` sets `passWithNoTests: true` with `include: packages/**/*.{test,spec}.{js,ts}`, so if that glob ever stops matching (a file moved or renamed, or the pattern edited) the step reports success over **zero** tests — measured, not inferred: a non-matching filter prints `No test files found, exiting with code 0` and exits 0. The suite's own guard against exactly this hazard, case (g) *"no `packages/*/vitest.config.*` sets `passWithNoTests`"*, iterates `listPackageDirs(PACKAGES_ROOT)` and therefore never inspects the **root** config — the one that actually sets the flag. The guard was written against the children while the flag lives in the parent. Mitigating and measured: the per-package path is strict — `npx vitest run` in `packages/web` prints `No test files found, exiting with code 1` and exits **1**, because each `packages/*/vitest.config.ts` deliberately omits `passWithNoTests` and carries a comment naming this false-green class. **Owed (audit M-13 follow-up, not yet ticketed):** case (g) must be extended to inspect the **root** `vitest.config.ts` — not only `packages/*` — so the false-green flag is caught where it actually lives; until then the root flag is unguarded and the per-package strictness above is the only backstop | `pnpm test` |

| Frozen-path effect check | `.claude/hooks/frozen-effect-check.sh --ci` | Every frozen path changed in the range is authorized **per commit** (`decisions/APPROVED-025` Option A, [DC-46]): for each non-merge commit between the base ref and HEAD, the frozen paths *that commit* changed must be covered by the `Scope:` of a ticket named in *that commit's own* message — a covering ticket named by a neighbouring commit vouches for nothing, which closes the one-scoped-commit-launders-the-branch gap (§12A effect-check limitation (6)) and removes the first-push special case. A **whole-range backstop** covers what per-commit attribution structurally cannot see: a merge commit's own conflict resolution can write frozen content belonging to no parent (an "evil merge") or discard a base fix ("take ours"), so any frozen path changed across `MERGE_BASE..HEAD` that no non-merge commit authored must be covered by `$CLAUDE_TICKET` or a ticket the merge commit itself names — a post-review [DC-46] hotfix after the first cut skipped merge commits and let an evil merge pass. Runs as its own stable-named `frozen-effect` CI job, and **is now a required status check** on `main` (CEO ruleset change, 28/07/2026) — established by a controlled A/B on one throwaway pull request (#5), not by reading a settings page. Same PR, same base, two head SHAs: with a failing `frozen-effect` check run the PR reported `mergeable_state: blocked`; with `frozen-effect` green and only `gates` still pending it reported `unstable`. `unstable` is returned only when nothing *blocking* is outstanding, which excludes a required-review explanation and confirms `gates` is not required, so the block on the first SHA is attributable to the required check. **Updated 28/07/2026:** `gates` is **now required too**, measured the same way on PR #7 — a failing `gates` with `frozen-effect` green reported `blocked`, where PR #5 had reported `unstable` for the same shape before the ruleset change. The measured chain: the advisory's affected range is **`≤ 5.0.7`, unbounded below**, first patched **5.0.8**, so the installed `brace-expansion@1.1.16` is genuinely in scope — this was checked, it is **not** a false positive of pnpm's range matching; the vulnerable path is `eslint@9.39.5 → @eslint/config-array@0.21.2 → minimatch@3.1.5 → brace-expansion@1.x`; **`9.39.5` is already the terminal 9.x release** and still pins `@eslint/config-array ^0.21.2`, so **no 9.x bump can clear it**; upstream fixed it only on the next major (`@eslint/config-array@0.23.5 → minimatch@^10.2.4 → brace-expansion@^5.0.8`), reachable only via **eslint 10** (latest `10.8.0`); and a direct override to 5.0.8 was tried at [SEC-5] time and **measured breaking eslint at require time** (`TypeError: expand is not a function` — 5.x exports `{expand}` named where `minimatch@3` requires a callable module), so the override route is closed while `minimatch@3` is in the tree. **What justifies suppressing a high:** a dev-time-only DoS in the lint toolchain — not shipped, not reachable from product code. (**One leg of this argument was removed 04/08/2026:** the clause used to end *"and `packages/` currently declares no production dependencies"*, which [DC-2] `ab8a186` made false — `packages/db` now declares `drizzle-orm@0.45.2` and `postgres@3.4.9`, the repo's first runtime dependencies, measured from the six `packages/*/package.json` files. The suppression still stands on its remaining terms: the vulnerable path is `eslint → @eslint/config-array → minimatch → brace-expansion`, reachable from no product runtime import. The "nothing ships at all" backstop is gone and does not come back.) **Trigger:** the ignore clears on the eslint 10 upgrade and nowhere else (`APPROVED-039` Option A / `SEC-9`'s deferred sibling), owed when `typescript-eslint` ships stable eslint-10 support. **Scoping is deliberate:** `ignoreGhsas` carries exactly one id, so a *second* advisory on this same path would still redden the gate. **Documented residue, added 04/08/2026 [DC-2] — not an ignore, a threshold pass:** `pnpm audit --json` now reports exactly one advisory below the gate's bar — `GHSA-67mh-4wv8-2f99` (**moderate**; esbuild dev-server reads any origin's request), on `packages__db > drizzle-kit > @esbuild-kit/esm-loader > @esbuild-kit/core-utils > esbuild@0.18.20`. Measured, not inferred: `pnpm audit --audit-level=high` exits **0** and the JSON metadata reads `{"moderate":1,"high":0,"critical":0}`, so nothing was ignored or configured away — the gate's `--audit-level=high` threshold simply does not judge it. Why it is accepted rather than fixed: `drizzle-kit` is a **devDependency** of `packages/db` (a generator, `drizzle.config.ts`, output redirected to gitignored `drizzle-kit-out/`), the advisory's code path is esbuild's **dev server**, which nothing in this repo starts, and the fix is not reachable by an override — the vulnerable `esbuild@0.18.20` is pinned inside the deprecated `@esbuild-kit/*` chain, while the tree's other esbuilds (`0.25.12`, `0.28.1`, vite/vitest) are already patched. **Revisit condition (falsifiable by looking, per `APPROVED-039` Option B's rule):** it clears when `drizzle-kit` drops `@esbuild-kit/*` (upstream merged that loader into `tsx`, which `drizzle-kit@0.31.10` *already* also depends on — it carries both) — check with `pnpm --filter @auto-mb/db why esbuild`, which today prints three versions, `0.18.20` under `@esbuild-kit/core-utils` and the patched `0.25.12`/`0.28.1` under `drizzle-kit`/`tsx` directly (root-scoped `pnpm why esbuild` sees only the root's `0.28.1` and will not show this — the wrong command answers "clear" every time). If the `@esbuild-kit` arm is gone and `pnpm audit` still reports the advisory, this residue is wrong and the row is owed a re-measurement. It also stops being a *residue* the moment the advisory is re-rated high/critical, at which point the gate reddens on its own. | SAST | semgrep 1.172.0 (engine pinned in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`) over `packages/**` with the repo-committed, pinned ruleset `.semgrep.yml` ([SEC-6], `decisions/APPROVED-030`) | No product code carries the enabled finding classes: **string-built** SQL query arguments (CWE-89 — the query-parameterization law, AGENTS.md non-negotiable 5) — inline concatenation/interpolation and, **since [SEC-7], the assign-then-pass, `.concat()` and array-`join` builder shapes** — `eval`/`new Function` (CWE-95; since [SEC-7] enabled `no-new-func`, this is defense-in-depth for *both*, behind SEC-4's lint bans), and `child_process` exec with concatenated/interpolated arguments (CWE-78), inline and assigned forms alike. **Widened 29/07/2026 [SEC-7]** (`decisions/APPROVED-037` Option A, closing F7; round 2 after the cross-model review): two taint-mode rules (`automb-sql-assigned-string-built-query`, `automb-command-injection-assigned-concat`) track a built string to the sink through intermediate variables and builders — `const q = "…" + x; db.query(q)`, the commonest real shape, now fires; every source requires a **non-constant operand**, and the concat sources additionally exclude `.concat()` results and nested `+` operands — when such an operand really carries taint, it originated at a `.concat()` argument or an inner literal-adjacent concat, each already a source, and propagation carries it to the sink (probe-guarded). **A `.join()` exclusion was tried in review round 3 and REVERTED in round 4** after the reviewer measured it silently dropping the caller-supplied-array class — `"… IN (" + ids.join(",") + ")"` where `ids` is a parameter has no in-function array literal for taint to originate at, so the exclusion cost 3 true positives the merged round-2 ruleset catches. The revert is a **deliberate FP-vs-FN choice, recorded not buried**: a literal-prefixed join over a provably-constant array (the const `clauses.push` assembly, the canonical parameterized IN-list builder, an all-literal array-join command) is syntactically identical to the hostile shape and semgrep cannot const-propagate arrays, so those shapes FIRE as **accepted false positives** — pinned in the probe as documentation cases (going quiet reddens the probe until the author proves the caller-supplied class still fires), rewrite: inline the constant fragments or use `= ANY($1)`. Same conservative doctrine as the kernel-segment lint rule ("renaming is cheaper than a hole"). What stays lawful and quiet, probe-asserted: parameterized + `execFile`, constant assign-then-pass, an all-literal array joined and passed **directly** (no literal-adjacent concat), const-propagated conditional reassembly (`q = q + " literal"`), module-const template, const base + const suffix, constant command. **Stated bounds:** taint enters array builders at array **literals** only (mutation/derivation/parameter arrays are not tracked as arrays — their `.join()` is caught only when spliced next to a literal), and a builder result passed directly to a sink with no literal-adjacent concat and no in-function array literal (`db.query(ids.join(","))`) is uncovered — true of the merged ruleset too, stated rather than implied. **Correction from the same review, recorded not glossed: an inline `// nosemgrep` comment in the scanned file CAN suppress a semgrep finding** — the previous claim that this gate "cannot be switched off from the scanned file" was false; both layers (lint + SAST) are suppressible by a reviewer-visible inline comment, and running the gate with `--disable-nosemgrep` is owed to a `package.json`-scoped follow-up (the `sast` invocation is frozen outside SEC-7's scope; proposed via `memory/inbox/`). Honest bounds: taint tracking is **intraprocedural** — a query string built in one function and passed to a sink in another stays uncovered; and semgrep 1.172.0 does not scan `.mts`/`.cts` files (measured), so for those extensions the [SEC-7] lint layer is the only automated cover. Rules are **vendored into `.semgrep.yml`**, adapted from semgrep-rules' security-audit families and mapped to OWASP/CWE in metadata — no registry fetch, no floating `latest` at scan time (`--metrics=off --disable-version-check`; the APPROVED-006 supply-chain posture). Runs **in the `pnpm gates` chain** (the implementer's measured call: scan ~2s, probe ~7s, well under the 60s budget — no new CI job, so no new required-check owner action). Fail-closed: missing binary → command-not-found non-zero; missing ruleset → semgrep config error non-zero; both probe-asserted. Probe: `scripts/probe-semgrep-gate.sh` — fixtures carrying each class drive the scan red with all **five** rule ids firing, and each assigned-form sub-class (assign+concat, assign+template, `.concat()`, array-join, **no-separator `.join()`** — a round-2 addition after the review measured `$ARR.join($SEP)` missing it) is pinned to its exact finding (check_id + fixture line — a rule id firing once could mask a blind sub-class, and a substring match would be the incidental-assertion class); the false-positive side is asserted in BOTH directions: the lawful-and-quiet shapes (parameterized + `execFile`, constant assign-then-pass, all-literal direct join, const-propagated reassembly, module-const template, const base + const suffix, constant command) each a control, and the **accepted-FP shapes pinned firing** as documentation (const `clauses.push` assembly, parameterized IN-list builder, all-literal array-join command — each with the gap message naming the proof obligation before accepting their silence); the caller-supplied-array class is pinned firing at three shapes (assigned IN-list, assigned exec join, inline IN-list), and a **tainted array joined then concatenated** guards the retained exclusions; plus the real `packages/` tree. **Sink-coverage bound, measured 04/08/2026 (audit of [DC-2] `ab8a186`; not closed by this row):** both SQL rules' sinks are `$DB.query` / `$DB.execute` / `$DB.raw` only, and [DC-2] introduced the repo's first raw-SQL execution sink under a *different* name — postgres.js `sql.unsafe()`, at its `await sql.unsafe(...)` call sites in `packages/db/src/migration-runner.ts` (**7**, unchanged at `ab8a186`, [DC-3] `5d45235` and the [DC-4] fan-in `65c2a24` — the "5" this cell claimed when it was written on 04/08/2026 was the auditor's own miscount off a `grep unsafe(` that counted doc-comment mentions; re-counted with `grep -c 'await sql.unsafe('` at both commits). Probe run in an isolated scratch file under `packages/db` (deleted after the run), the SAME template-literal payload — an interpolated `table` identifier — on two adjacent lines: passed to `db.execute(...)` it is **2 findings** (`automb-sql-string-built-query` and `automb-sql-assigned-string-built-query`, both reported at that line); passed to `sql.unsafe(...)` it is **0 findings**. So the CWE-89 claim above is a claim about the *named sinks*, not about all raw-SQL execution in product code, and a `.unsafe` sink is exactly what a future feature-code author would reach for. Owed: add `$DB.unsafe($Q, ...)` to both rules' `pattern-sinks` with a probe case per rule (`.semgrep.yml` is a frozen path — needs its own ticket + scoped decision; named here, not filed) | `pnpm sast`, `pnpm sast:probe` |

| **CI runner** | `.github/workflows/ci.yml` | **Present. Half-enforcing — measured 27/07/2026, not inferred from docs.** (a) A repository rule *does* block direct pushes to `main`: `git push origin main` was rejected with "push declined due to repository rule violations", so the "only reviewed merges touch main" property (DESIGN.md §9) is real and enforced by the forge, not by agent good behaviour. (b) The CI check is **not a required status check**: PR #3 merged while its `pull_request` run (#38) was still `in_progress`. So a PR can land with gates unfinished or red. The last completed run on the merged tree (#37, `2415544`) was green, but that was luck of timing, not a gate. **Updated 28/07/2026:** (b) is now half-closed. The CEO marked **`frozen-effect`** a required status check in the ruleset, established by a controlled A/B on PR #5 (failing `frozen-effect` → `blocked`; green `frozen-effect` with `gates` pending → `unstable`, which excludes both a required-review explanation and a required `gates`). **Closed 28/07/2026 — `gates` is now required as well**, so (b) is fully closed and this row's original finding no longer stands. Measured on throwaway PR #7 rather than read off the ruleset UI: one file carrying a deliberate `no-const-assign` error drove `gates` to fail at the lint step while `frozen-effect` stayed green, and the PR reported `mergeable_state: blocked`. The control is PR #5, which reported `unstable` for the same shape (`frozen-effect` green, `gates` not passing) *before* the ruleset change — and `unstable` is returned only when nothing blocking is outstanding, so a non-required failing check cannot produce `blocked`. A PR can therefore no longer land with lint, typecheck, Vitest, secretlint, the 310-case hook suite or `routing-check` red or unfinished. **Remaining owner action:** none on required checks. Note that **no pull-request review is required** on `main`: PR #5's `unstable` reading proves an unreviewed PR is mergeable once the required checks are green, so DESIGN.md §9's "only reviewed merges touch main" is enforced only in the weak sense that direct pushes are refused — the *review* half is convention. **Corrected 29/07/2026 [SEC-8]** (`decisions/APPROVED-038` Option C): that finding stands, but the remedy `[SEC-3f]` prescribed — "the CEO flips require-pull-request-review" — **cannot be executed on this repository**, and carrying it as one click away was false from the day it was written. Measured: the repo has exactly **one collaborator** (`punyanagari`, admin — `list_repository_collaborators`) and every PR is authored by that same account, because agents push and open PRs through the CEO's credential; **GitHub does not permit approving your own pull request**. Requiring ≥1 approval therefore makes *every* PR unmergeable except by admin bypass, and an admin bypass on the sole account is the advertised-control-that-does-nothing shape `APPROVED-012` rejects. What actually carries this guarantee is named in the row below |

The exposure is unchanged and stays visible: an unreviewed PR *can* merge once the required checks are green. What changed is the remedy, because the prescribed one is impossible here (single account, self-approval prohibited — see the CI-runner row). The control that actually carries this: **every PR touching a governed surface — `packages/kernel/**`, `.claude/**`, `docs/js/**` (§12A), the gate scripts, or any frozen path — is reviewed by an agent on a MODEL DIFFERENT from the diff's author** before merge. **Its weakness, stated rather than glossed:** this is orchestration convention enforced by AGENTS.md, **not** by the forge — a session that ignores AGENTS.md can skip it, and nothing mechanical would notice. **Its evidence, measured not asserted:** on 29/07 that hop found a CRITICAL regression (the DC-46 merge-carried frozen-path bypass, reproduced in two subclasses) **on a PR that had already merged green with every required status check passing** — which forge-required review would not have caught, since the sole human would have clicked approve on a green PR. Wiring either check into the chain edits the frozen `package.json` and stays owed to a scoped decision (`decisions/APPROVED-032` "Owed wiring") — until then the lint runs only by hand or as OPS-1's verify. **The exposure stopped being hypothetical on 04/08/2026:** [DC-3] `5d45235` put the first three real hand-written migrations in `packages/db/migrations/` (`0001_uuidv7_function.sql`, `0002_create_tenants.sql`, `0003_create_audit_log.sql`), so the "no-migrations-dir tolerance" branch above no longer describes this repo. A hand run passes — `bash scripts/migration-lint.sh` printed `checked … (3 migration file(s); packages/db/migrations found)` / `OK`, exit 0 — but that is a hand measurement with no gate behind it, and the next migration to land is unlinted unless the wiring decision arrives first. **Re-measured after wave 5 (`c266605`): 13 files, still `OK`, still exit 0, still by hand** — ten more migrations (`0010`, `0011`, `0020`, `0021`, `0030`-`0032`, `0040`, `0050`, `0060`) landed unlinted-by-any-gate across three waves, which is the shape this row's "still un-wired" finding predicts and no longer merely warns about. The hand runs keep passing, which is exactly what makes the missing wiring easy to defer. Squawk itself remains absent — `tickets/DC-2.md` defers to it while nothing tracks its adoption (found by the APPROVED-032 research pass) |
| Harness portability | `scripts/portability-check.sh` + `scripts/probe-portability-check.sh` | **Wired 29/07/2026 [MIG-2] — this row's finding is closed; the live row is in §2.1.** Was: written and proven under `decisions/APPROVED-033` [MIG-1] (8-plant probe; first live run caught `LITELLM_LOG` absent from the manifest) but NOT in the chain, because wiring edits the frozen `package.json`. `decisions/APPROVED-034` (Option A) authorized the wiring and it landed under a bound session, fault-injection-proven at the `portability:ci` step. Rule 9's non-mechanical remainder stays review law, unchanged |
| DAST | OWASP ZAP baseline vs dev deployment | Not installed; scheduled Stage 5 — the one row here whose absence is on plan rather than an overclaim |

## 5. V8 Authorization (the product's core risk)

| ASVS | Requirement | Implementation | Proving test | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 8.2.1 | Deny by default; every route guarded | Kernel authorizer guard on all routes | Route-coverage test: unguarded route fails CI | TBD |
| 8.3.1 | Permission dependency-graph closure enforced server-side both directions | Matrix save handler | Unit: broken combination impossible to store | TBD |
| 8.2.2 | Work-scoped access on every read path (lists, details, reports, dashboard, serial lookup) | Scope filter in query layer + RLS | Integration suite ported from v1 work-scope tests | TBD |
| 8.4.1 | **Tenant isolation** | PostgreSQL RLS on every tenant table; tenant context bound per request/transaction. **The DATABASE half landed 04/08/2026 [DC-4] `2b55b12` (merged `65c2a24`); the BINDING half has not, and the status cell reflects the pair, not the half.** `migrations/0011_audit_log_rls.sql`: `ENABLE` **and** `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` on the partitioned `audit_log` parent (FORCE is what stops the table owner reading past the policy — without it isolation silently does not exist), plus policy `audit_log_tenant_isolation` whose `USING` **and** `WITH CHECK` are both `tenant_id = (select nullif(current_setting('app.tenant_id', true), ''))::uuid` — the `(select …)` wrap so the planner can hoist it to a once-per-statement InitPlan rather than re-invoke a STABLE function per row (**proof level, stated because it is weaker than it sounds:** the suite asserts the stored expression's SHAPE — `pg_get_expr(polqual)`/`pg_get_expr(polwithcheck)` each contain `select` and `current_setting` — it does not read a query plan; no `EXPLAIN`- or InitPlan-based assertion exists anywhere in this tree, grepped, so once-per-statement evaluation is designed and argued in the migration header, not measured), `missing_ok = true` so an unset GUC reads NULL instead of raising, and `nullif(…, '')` so an explicitly-blank context folds to NULL as well. Either NULL path makes the predicate NULL, which `USING` and `WITH CHECK` both treat as reject: zero rows, INSERT refused, never a default tenant. `migrations/0010_create_app_role.sql`: `automb_app` `NOLOGIN NOBYPASSRLS`, owner of nothing, granted `SELECT, INSERT` on `audit_log` and never `UPDATE`/`DELETE`/`TRUNCATE` (so the append-only trail is now an ACL, not only a comment); `0011` additionally `REVOKE EXECUTE`s both partition routines from `PUBLIC`. Proof: `packages/db/test/rls-coverage.dbtest.ts` (21 tests, catalog-driven over every non-system schema with **no allowlist**, so a future `tenant_id`-bearing table with no RLS migration fails it automatically) — effect-level, driving real statements as `automb_app` via `SET SESSION AUTHORIZATION`: no context → zero rows and rejected INSERT; wrong-tenant INSERT rejected by `WITH CHECK`; a partition created AFTER the policy still enforces it; `rolbypassrls` false; owns no relation; the three violation classes (RLS off / FORCE off / no policy) planted as isolated negative controls and detected for their own reason, then dropped and re-asserted clean; a `tenant_id`-carrying materialized view and foreign table flagged by EXISTENCE (their `relrowsecurity` is meaningless); and a `SET ROLE` scan over `migrations/` and `src/` (CVE-2024-10976 class, ARCHITECTURE.md §5). **Deliberate GLOBAL exclusion, named rather than omitted (`tickets/DC-45.md` criterion 4, [DC-45] `af8f556`):** `units` (and its alias rows) is global reference vocabulary with no `tenant_id` by contract — it is listed in that suite's `GLOBAL_REFERENCE_TABLES` registry, which asserts each named table genuinely has no `tenant_id` column and carries a self-check proving that assertion is not vacuous; `units-master.dbtest.ts` asserts the same from its own side. So the exclusion is a documented assertion, not a silent gap. **Extended 05/08/2026 to the whole tenant-scoped schema — [DC-13] `1de15eb` (`works`, `schedules`, `work_items`) and [DC-47] `4018ff2` (`contacts`), merged `a28e849`.** Each of the four lands `ENABLE` + `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` + its own `<table>_tenant_isolation` policy **in the same migration that creates the table** (`0030`/`0031`/`0032`/`0040`), with the predicate copied verbatim from `0011` — so there is no intermediate migration in which a tenant table exists unprotected, which is the window a create-now-protect-later split would open. Grants are `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE` and never `DELETE` (soft-delete via `deleted_at` on `works`, retire-never-delete on `contacts`), and `0021` grants `automb_app` read-only access to the global `units`/`unit_aliases`. **The composite tenant FK is part of the isolation story, not schema hygiene** (a DC-13 review MAJOR): RLS cannot close cross-tenant FK smuggling, because a policy's `WITH CHECK` only validates the NEW row's own `tenant_id` while FK validation runs as an internal system check that does not apply RLS to the parent lookup — so a tenant-A row could reference a tenant-B parent by id. The fix is a `UNIQUE (tenant_id, id)` on each parent plus composite children — `schedules.FOREIGN KEY (tenant_id, work_id) REFERENCES works (tenant_id, id)` and `work_items.FOREIGN KEY (tenant_id, schedule_id) REFERENCES schedules (tenant_id, id)` — which makes the parent's tenant part of the referenced key, so the smuggling row has no valid target. Re-measured live for this audit as `automb_app` with tenant A's context: inserting a schedule whose `work_id` belongs to tenant B fails with `violates foreign key constraint "schedules_tenant_id_work_id_fkey"`, while the same insert against A's own work succeeds — the constraint discriminates, it does not blanket-deny. (`work_items.unit_id` stays a plain `REFERENCES units (id)`: `units` is the global, non-tenant table above, so there is no tenant to carry.) The 21-test coverage suite needed no allowlist edit for any of the four — that is the no-allowlist property paying out. **Three bounds, all measured for this row, none of which the cell above should be read past.** (1) **The context is self-service until DC-6.** Any session can `SET app.tenant_id` to any value; nothing kernel-side binds it per request/transaction, so today the policy separates *contexts*, not *callers*. DC-6 binds it (`SET LOCAL`, never `SET ROLE`) and DC-7 probes it through a pooled connection — a policy nobody binds a context for protects nothing, which is why this row stays `TBD`. (2) **Partition-direct access is outside the RLS layer.** Measured on a live cluster for this audit: `audit_log` reads `relrowsecurity=t, relforcerowsecurity=t` while its partition `audit_log_2031_06` reads `f, f`, and the coverage query deliberately excludes `relispartition` children. What closes it today is the ACL, not RLS — `has_table_privilege('automb_app', 'audit_log_2031_06', 'SELECT')` is **false**, and as `automb_app` a direct `SELECT … FROM audit_log_2031_06` returns `permission denied for table audit_log_2031_06` (with no context and with a wrong-tenant context alike), while the same session through the parent returns 0 rows. A future migration that GRANTs on a partition would reopen it and neither the policy nor the coverage query would notice. (3) **The proving suite is a `.dbtest.ts`, which no gate runs** (§2.1) — it is DC-4's `verify:` line, green on demand (21/21), not on merge | Cross-tenant probe suite: every endpoint, foreign tenant id → 404/denied | TBD |
| N/A (business rule; loosely 8.2.1) | Owner/master-admin guards (cannot disable/demote/strip; no self-disable) | Server-side guards | Unit + integration | TBD |
| 2.3.4 | Approvals: atomic claim, first decision wins, re-validation at apply | Approvals module (kernel audit writer in same tx) | Concurrency test: two deciders → one wins | TBD |

## 6. V2 Validation & Business Logic

The business invariants (PRODUCT-SPEC §6 R1–R19) are security-relevant:
they are the integrity guarantees the audit certifies.

| ASVS | Requirement | Implementation | Proving test | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2.3.4 | Gap-free numbering under concurrency; cancelled numbers never reused | `assignNumber(tx, scope)` — serialized per scope (advisory lock). **The PRECONDITION landed 05/08/2026 [DC-15] `fd41517`, and only the precondition:** challan numbers are globally unique across works only if a work code is never reused, so `migrations/0060_work_code_letter_number_unique.sql` adds `works_tenant_id_work_code_key` and `works_tenant_id_letter_number_key` as **plain, non-partial** `UNIQUE` table constraints — deliberately not `WHERE deleted_at IS NULL`, so a soft-deleted work keeps its code forever and cannot free it for a new work whose `<work_code>-DC` prefix (PRODUCT-SPEC.md §7) would then collide. The constraint FORM is the guarantee: Postgres offers no `WHERE` clause on a table constraint at all, so "not partial" is structural rather than a `CREATE INDEX` someone must keep `WHERE`-less. Proven by `packages/db/test/work-code-uniqueness.dbtest.ts`, which asserts `indpred IS NULL` on both (not partial, read from the catalog), that soft-delete does not free either value, that the same pair IS accepted under a different tenant (per-tenant, not global), and — the case that makes the choice auditable rather than asserted — a demonstration that a scratch `WHERE deleted_at IS NULL` partial index WRONGLY allows the reuse, rolled back so it is never left live, immediately followed by the real plain constraint rejecting the identical pattern. `packages/works/src/challan-prefix.ts`'s `deriveChallanPrefix` is a pure derivation with no uniqueness logic of its own; its "taken" question is answered by that constraint, proven by effect in `packages/works/test/work-code-service.dbtest.ts` (derive a prefix from a soft-deleted work, then show the code cannot be re-created). **Status stays `TBD`: none of this is the numbering control.** `assignNumber` does not exist, no advisory lock is taken anywhere, and the parallel-issue concurrency suite (DC-29/DC-30, kernel) is the row's actual proving test | Concurrency: parallel issue → no dup/skip | TBD |
| 2.3.4 | One-open-draft rules under concurrency | Conditional unique constraints | Concurrency: parallel draft → exactly one | TBD |
| N/A (business rule; cf 2.3.2) | Delivery/installation/PAC caps, amendment floor | Service-layer validation + DB constraints. **Recorded 05/08/2026 [DC-14] `d997cc4` because the cap arithmetic is only as correct as the figure it reads:** `works` now carries `advertised_value` (the tender figure) and `contract_value` (the net-bid figure) as separate columns, and `packages/db/src/works.ts` makes `workContractValue(work)` the single sanctioned ceiling accessor — no caller may decide "which column is the ceiling" for itself. This is a correctness precondition, not a control: under `pricing_shape = 'letter_percentage'` the contract value is **not** recoverable by summing `work_items.effective_amount`, because Shape A prints per-item amounts at the ADVERTISED rate, and `packages/db/test/works-pricing-shape.dbtest.ts` proves it on the real corpus (the PL275-shaped case, a 29% divergence between Σ(items) and the printed contract value). A cap computed off an item-sum, or off `advertised_value`, would therefore be wrong by that margin while looking arithmetically sound. Money stays `numeric(18,2)` read as `string` end-to-end, never coerced through a float64. **Extended 05/08/2026 [DC-24] `713a7e7` — the same chain of custody, one link earlier:** `workContractValue()` decides which COLUMN is the ceiling; DC-24's classifier decides what VALUE that column receives when the letter's printed Net Bid and the computed total disagree. Manager ruling applied (`5d7023d`, PRODUCT-SPEC §5.1.3): the printed figure is the datum and the computation its checksum, so within a one-paisa tolerance `contract_value` carries the PRINTED Net Bid, never the computed figure, and the divergence is not swallowed — a `totals_rounding_divergence` flag carries `{ printed, computed, diff }` plus the raw totals block. All six corpus letters reconcile exactly (`diff = 0`, no flag), so the tolerance band is defensive rather than corpus-exercised. [DC-26] `6efc465` is the integrity half of the same guarantee (PRODUCT-SPEC §5.1 "never discards information"): six proven traps raise `needsReview` rather than let a wrong figure through silently — including the PL280 corrigendum that restates a unit in prose only, a four-fold quantity error if trusted, which is a cap input. Both are pure-library extraction, so nothing here writes a cap or a work: the parser package exports no work-writing function and imports no database (asserted by source-scan in `needs-review.test.ts`). The caps themselves — delivery, installation, PAC, amendment floor — remain unbuilt | Ported v1 unit + integration suites | TBD |
| N/A (business rule; cf 2.3.3) | Idempotency on mutating endpoints | Kernel idempotency-key interceptor | Integration: replay returns first result | TBD |
| 2.2.1, 2.2.2 | Server-side date/quantity validation (no future dates, ≥ LOA date, positive qty) | Zod/DTO validation + service checks | Unit matrix | TBD |

## 9. V4 API & Web Service

| ASVS | Requirement | Implementation | Proving test | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2.2.1, 15.3.3 | All input validated against schemas; unknown fields stripped | DTO validation at the edge | Unit | TBD |
| 1.2.4 | Parameterized data access only | Drizzle; no raw interpolation. **First real mechanism landed 04/08/2026 [DC-2] `ab8a186`, verified by probe for this row (status deliberately unmoved — the Proving-test cell still names a gate that does not exist, §2.2):** (a) the application-facing factory `createDbClient` (`packages/db/src/client.ts`) returns `{ db, serverVersion, close }` where `db` is Drizzle's parameterized query builder — the postgres.js escape hatches are not statically reachable through it: a probe file asserting `client.unsafe(...)` and `client.db.$client` compiled only with `@ts-expect-error` on both, i.e. both are type errors under `pnpm --filter @auto-mb/db typecheck`. (b) The `.unsafe()`-carrying connection (`createMigrationConnection`) is **off the package root**: `packages/db/src/index.ts` does not re-export it and `packages/db/package.json` exposes it only at the `@auto-mb/db/migrate` subpath — probed by adding a root import of it, which fails typecheck with `TS2305: Module '"../src/index.js"' has no exported member 'createMigrationConnection'` (exit 2). (c) The runner (`packages/db/src/migration-runner.ts`) splices **no caller-supplied value** into statement text: per-call values (filename, checksum, advisory-lock key, `to_regclass` arguments) are bound through postgres.js tagged templates, the tracking-table identifier comes from a module constant via the `sql()` identifier helper, and the only two sites that splice text into a statement at all are both trusted-static — the `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` tracking-table bootstrap (module-constant name) and the `DROP INDEX CONCURRENTLY IF EXISTS` crash-recovery statement, whose index name is parsed out of the migration file's own committed text and re-quoted by `quotePgIdentifier`, which doubles embedded `"`. The remaining `sql.unsafe()` calls take either a literal (`BEGIN`/`COMMIT`/`ROLLBACK`) or the migration file's own committed DDL, which cannot be parameterized at all — the whole body on the transactional path (`sql.unsafe(migration.sql)`) and, since [DC-3] `5d45235`, one call per statement on the non-transactional path. **Re-verified against that DC-3 refactor, because "executes a slice" is where a rewrite could creep in:** each slice is `sqlText.slice(start, end).trim()` of the ORIGINAL file, with the boundaries found on a length-preserving stripped copy (comments and dollar-quoted bodies overwritten space-for-space, so offsets still line up). The stripper therefore decides only where statements END, never what TEXT is sent; no statement is reassembled from the stripped form. **Bound, so the cell is not read as more than it proves:** Drizzle's own `db.execute()` accepts a raw `string` (drizzle-orm 0.45.2 `pg-core/db.js`: `typeof query === 'string' ? sql.raw(query)`) and that overload IS on this surface — measured type-legal — so "parameterized-only" is a property of how the surface is *used*, enforced today by SAST on string-built `.execute` arguments (§2.1) and not by the type system; the `.unsafe` sink is outside that SAST rule's sink list (§2.1 SAST row, sink-coverage bound) | Static query audit (§2) | TBD |
| 16.5.1 | Uniform error contract; no stack traces/internal detail to clients | Error filter. **The first service-layer error surface landed 05/08/2026 [DC-15] `fd41517` (`packages/works/src/errors.ts`), and it comes with a measured trap for whoever builds the filter (DC-17/DC-27).** What is right already: the three typed errors carry a machine-readable `.code` plus a fixed, generic `.message` naming no constraint and no SQL — verified by probe, a duplicate work code raises `code: 'work_code_conflict'` with message `"A work with this work code already exists for this tenant."` and nothing else. `.existingWorkId` is resolved by a lookup that deliberately includes soft-deleted rows so a UI can offer undelete, and it is **tenant-scoped by effect**, re-measured here rather than taken from the review: with the same `work_code` present under tenants A and B, the conflict raised for A carried A's work id (`isA=true, isB=false`), so the field cannot become a cross-tenant existence oracle. **The trap — `.message` on the UNMAPPED path is not generic, measured on a live cluster for this audit:** any error the service does not map (probed with a foreign-key violation) is rethrown as drizzle-orm's own wrapper, whose message begins `Failed query:` and then **embeds the full INSERT statement**, table and column names and `$n` placeholders included; the outer error's `.name` is plain `Error` and its `.code` is `undefined` (the SQLSTATE lives on the wrapped cause, which is why the service's own mapping unwraps it). So an API that serializes `err.message` for anything it does not recognize ships the query structure to the client — exactly this row's finding class. The error filter must allowlist by error TYPE (`.code`/`instanceof`), never pass an unrecognized `.message` through, and this row cannot go `PARTIAL` until an integration snapshot proves an unmapped failure renders a generic body. **One route into that path was closed 05/08/2026 (`6d91c33`, [DC-15] follow-up to this audit's finding):** `createWork` branched on the `PricingShape` TS union, so any runtime string other than `'letter_percentage'` — an HTTP body's, once DC-17 lands — fell into the `per_schedule` arm, passed the service mirror, and died at the DB `CHECK` as one of these SQL-embedding unmapped errors. It now rejects an unrecognised `pricingShape` up front with a typed error naming the received value. That closes one known route, not the class: any unanticipated Postgres error still reaches the caller with the query text in `.message` | Integration snapshot | TBD |
| 6.3.1, 2.4.1 | Rate limiting on login & expensive endpoints, shared across instances | Redis-backed limiter (or edge) | Integration | TBD |

## 11. V16 Logging & Error Handling / Audit

| ASVS | Requirement | Implementation | Proving test | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 16.2.1, 16.2.5 | Every request logged with request-id (echoed to client), method, path, status, duration, user id — never bodies/secrets | Log middleware | Log-capture test | TBD |
| 16.3.3 (same-tx atomicity is a business invariant) | Audit entry for every create/update/cancel/delete/permission change, **in the same transaction** as the mutation | Kernel audit writer. **The table this row writes to landed 04/08/2026 [DC-3] `5d45235`; the writer did NOT.** `packages/db/migrations/0003_create_audit_log.sql` creates `audit_log` — columns `id uuid DEFAULT uuidv7()`, `tenant_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES tenants (id)`, `occurred_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now()`, `actor uuid NOT NULL`, `action text`, `entity_type text`, `entity_id uuid`, `details jsonb` — `PARTITION BY RANGE (occurred_at)`, monthly, with `PRIMARY KEY (tenant_id, occurred_at, id)` (tenant_id leads the PK's own backing index per ARCHITECTURE.md §4 item 1, while Postgres's rule that a partitioned table's PK must *include* the partition key is satisfied by membership, not by leading). Partition bounds are pinned to **UTC** — the routine appends a literal ` 00:00:00+00` to each bound rather than letting a bare date literal resolve against the creating session's `TimeZone` GUC (the review measured an Asia/Kolkata session and a UTC session producing bounds 5.5h apart for the same month; `packages/db/test/audit-log-partition-tz.dbtest.ts` is the regression). The partition routine ships in the **same migration** (`ensure_audit_log_partition(date)` plus the zero-arg `create_next_audit_log_partition()`), seeding the current and next month. **What this row still does not have, stated so the cell is not misread:** no writer, no same-transaction coupling, and nothing yet calls the routine on a schedule — the writer is kernel work (DC-33) and the scheduled call is DC-33/worker scope; the migration grants and revokes no privilege, so the "no UPDATE/DELETE path for the application role" discipline the migration header records is *documentation*, not an enforced control, until DC-4's RLS policies land. **That last clause is discharged as of 04/08/2026 [DC-4] `2b55b12`:** `migrations/0010_create_app_role.sql` grants `automb_app` exactly `SELECT, INSERT` on `audit_log`, and `rls-coverage.dbtest.ts` asserts `UPDATE`, `DELETE` and `TRUNCATE` all read false for that role — the append-only property is now an ACL a test reads from the live catalog, not a comment. The writer, the same-transaction coupling and the scheduled partition call remain unbuilt (DC-33), and the ACL binds only `automb_app`. The migration-running role is unaffected by both the ACL and the policy — measured on a live cluster for this audit: as that role (a superuser in the test harness) a no-context `SELECT` through `audit_log` returned rows, where the same statement as `automb_app` returned zero, `FORCE` closing the OWNER bypass but nothing closing the SUPERUSER one. So "append-only" and "non-erasable" are proven for the application role and remain operational discipline for the deploy/migration role | Integration: rollback removes both | TBD |
| N/A (business rule) | Cancels/destructive edits require human note | Service validation | Unit | TBD |
| 8.4.1, 16.4.2 | Logs/audit are tenant-scoped on read | Audit viewer authz. **Schema precondition only, landed 04/08/2026 [DC-3] `5d45235` — no enforcement yet.** `audit_log.tenant_id` is `uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES tenants (id)`, so every audit row is attributable to exactly one tenant and an untenanted row cannot be inserted; and because `tenant_id` LEADS the primary key `(tenant_id, occurred_at, id)`, the predicate DC-4's RLS policy will add is served by the PK's own backing index rather than forcing a scan (ARCHITECTURE.md §4 item 1 — an index that does not lead with `tenant_id` will not be used). `packages/db/test/schema-conventions.dbtest.ts` enforces both properties catalog-wide, not for this table specifically, so a later table cannot regress the convention silently. **Superseded 04/08/2026 by [DC-4] `2b55b12`** — this cell previously read "no RLS, no policy, no grant/revoke anywhere", which the merge made false: `audit_log` now carries `ENABLE` + `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` and a `USING`/`WITH CHECK` policy keyed on `app.tenant_id`, and `automb_app` holds `SELECT, INSERT` only (§5 row 8.4.1 carries the full mechanism and its three bounds — do not duplicate the claim here). **What that does and does not buy this row:** the *read* half of 16.4.2 is now enforced at the database for any caller connecting as `automb_app` with a context set — a cross-tenant read returns zero rows rather than rows. It is NOT yet tenant-scoping in the sense this row certifies, because nothing binds the context per request (DC-6) and no audit VIEWER exists to be authorized (no endpoint, no query path, no UI) — so the row's own subject, the viewer's authz, remains entirely unbuilt | Integration | TBD |
| N/A (operational; cf 16.2.1) | Operational alerting derived from the request-log stream: error rate, p99 by `endpointClass`, saturation, job-queue depth/oldest-age, failed-login spikes — alert → ticket with a verify line, no pagers (`decisions/APPROVED-032` items 1/6) | CloudWatch Alarms over EMF/log metric filters; `endpointClass` field added to the log line this section already mandates; SLO defaults in `docs/slo.md` (OPS-4) | Launch track: an alarm-set existence check in the deploy pipeline (future, named when the pipeline exists) | TBD |

| 15.1.2, 15.2.1 | Pinned lockfiles; SCA in CI | `pnpm-lock.yaml` (pinned, `--frozen-lockfile` in CI) + SCA gate (§2.1, [SEC-5]): `pnpm audit --audit-level=high` in `pnpm gates` | `pnpm sca` in the chain; discrimination + fail-closed-offline proven by `scripts/probe-sca-gate.sh` (`pnpm sca:probe`) | TBD |
| N/A (operational; cf 15.2.3 — no container control in ASVS 5.0) | Hardened, minimal container images | Distroless/slim base; Trivy | CI | TBD |
| N/A (legal, India DPDP 2023; cf 14.1.1) | DPDP Act 2023 artifacts (notice, consent, retention, per-tenant export/erasure path) | Launch-track deliverable. **The SCHEMA half landed 04/08/2026 [DC-3] `5d45235` (its criterion 5) — the targeting mechanism, not the path.** Personal-data columns are tagged at schema time by a `COMMENT ON COLUMN` whose text starts `pii:` (e.g. `pii:contact`), which a future export/erasure job enumerates straight from the catalog via `pg_catalog.col_description` / `information_schema.columns`. Mechanism choice is documented with its reason in `packages/db/README.md` §4: the comment travels with the column through `pg_dump`/restore and to every replica, needs no join and no second table to keep in sync, and cannot drift from the actual column set the way a registry table or an application-level annotation can. `packages/db/test/schema-conventions.dbtest.ts` proves it **both directions** in one negative-control case: a planted `contact_name` with no comment is reported by the untagged-PII catalog query, while its planted sibling `contact_email` commented `pii:contact` is asserted NOT reported (so the check reads the comment, not the name pattern), then both throwaway tables are dropped and every coverage query is re-asserted empty. **Superseded 05/08/2026 — the mechanism has met live data.** This cell said "no real column is tagged today … proven on planted columns"; [DC-47] `4018ff2` (merged `a28e849`) created `contacts` with **four** genuinely-tagged columns, read back from a live cluster for this audit via `col_description(…) LIKE 'pii:%'`: `contacts.contact_person_name`, `contacts.contact_person_designation`, `contacts.contact_person_phone`, `contacts.contact_person_email` — exactly ARCHITECTURE.md §7's named personal-data set, on exactly the Contacts master the old text anticipated. The surrounding columns are tagged NOT-personal on purpose and say so in their own comments (`contacts.designation` is the office's own post title, `station`, `bank_details`, and `tenants.name`/`units.name` are organisation or reference identities), so an erasure job targeting `pii:` prefixes gets the contact person and not the counterparty organisation. **Measured gate bound, owed not hidden (DC-47 review NIT-2):** the conventions suite polices only the column-NAME patterns `%_name` / `%_email` / `%_phone`, so of those four columns it can enforce three. `contact_person_designation` ends in neither pattern, so it is tagged **by author discipline, not by any gate** — deleting its `pii:` prefix reddens nothing, and the migration's own column comment states this rather than leaving it to be discovered. Owed: extend the conventions suite's personal-data predicate beyond the three suffixes (a `%_designation` arm at minimum, better a per-table declared set) so the tag is mechanically load-bearing everywhere it is applied. Until then the export/erasure *targeting* is only as complete as the tagging discipline the gate does not check. The per-tenant export/erasure path itself, the notice/consent artifacts and the retention schedule are all still owed, unchanged in scope by this | Checklist + export test | TBD (launch track) |
| N/A (legal, India DPDP 2023; cf 14.1.1) | Personal data committed to the source repository — recorded, not remediated | **Deliberate CEO decision, `tickets/DC-22.md` §"Decided — CEO, 2026-07-28", landed 04/08/2026 as [DC-22] `5072786`.** Officer names are RETAINED verbatim rather than redacted: the point of the field is to record *who issued the LOA*, which a role designation alone cannot do, so corpus and schema keep the real name as a `(name, role)` pair (`signatory`, `officer_in_charge`, `consignee[n]`). What is in git as of that commit, read off the files rather than off the ticket: six `pdftotext -layout` text extractions under `packages/loa/fixtures/` (`PL270-CRB`, `PL273-JHS`, `PL275-BKN`, `PL276-GTL`, `PL280-ADI`, `PL281-BB`), each carrying one named signing officer with role designation plus that letter's `Digitally Signed` / `View Signature Details` signature-metadata marker (e.g. `NARENDRA SINGH`, `Sr.DSTE/CO`), and each carrying the counterparty firm's name and postal address; `fixtures/corpus.json` declares `"redaction": "verbatim-names-retained"` on all six entries. No email addresses appear in the six files (grepped; the only phone-shaped digit runs are letter and tender numbers). **One-way door, acknowledged in the ticket:** once in git history, removal is a history rewrite, not a delete. **Why the export/erasure obligations in the row above are unaffected:** this is corpus/regression data in the repository, not tenant runtime data in the product's database — no Data Principal reaches it through the product and nothing here is served to a tenant, so the per-tenant export/erasure path that row owes is unchanged in scope by it. Two things this row does **not** claim: that a DPDP notice/consent basis for holding the fixtures has been established (not assessed), and that the same choice extends to production works data (it does not — that is the row above, still `TBD`) | `packages/loa/test/corpus-manifest.test.ts` pins the *declared* state, not compliance: cases "every entry declares a redaction mode", "declares the same redaction mode consistently ('verbatim-names-retained', per the CEO decision)" and the per-fixture SHA-256 verbatimness guard, so a silent redaction-policy change or a fixture edit reddens `pnpm test` (in the chain, §2.1). No test can prove this row's legal question | N/A (no product control governs source-repo fixture data; the exposure is accepted by CEO decision and recorded here so no later reader mistakes it for an oversight — the tenant-runtime DPDP path stays `TBD (launch track)` in the row above) |
