## 12A. Org tooling (outside certified product scope)

These rows cover **internal org tooling only** — the static GitHub Pages CEO
dashboard in `docs/` (plain ES modules, no build step, operated by the CEO in
their own browser). It is **not part of the certified Auto-MB product surface**
and is out of audit scope; ASVS IDs below are cited as the nearest analogue,
not as certified product controls. It is listed because AGENTS.md rule 1 says
"module" without qualification, and because the dashboard sits on the
**governance control path**: it writes `decisions/*.md`, and a bound
`APPROVED-*kernel*.md` decision file is precisely what lifts the
`packages/kernel` freeze in `.claude/hooks/guard.sh` (bound, since
`decisions/APPROVED-013`, to a set `$CLAUDE_TICKET` named on a whole-token
`Authorizes:` line of an `APPROVED` decision whose `Scope:` line covers the
path — never to bare filename existence, and never to a branch diff; an unset
`$CLAUDE_TICKET` always blocks). **Correction, 28/07/2026:** "on a whole-token
`Authorizes:` line" overstates the binding. Both lines are located by
line-anchored greps over the *entire* decision file, so they bind from inside
an HTML comment, a fenced code block or a prose counter-example just as well as
from the header — see the markdown-blind row in the table below, and
`decisions/APPROVED-023`. **Corrected again later the same day:** APPROVED-023
(Option A) landed — both fields are now read solely from the decision's header
block (`decision_header_scan` in `.claude/hooks/frozen-paths.lib.sh`), so the
original claim finally holds, and holds stronger: the binding is to header
fields, not to matching lines wherever they appear.

| ASVS | Requirement | Implementation | Proving test | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 14.3.3, 14.3.1 | CEO PAT held only in browser storage and transmitted only to `api.github.com`; guidance mandates a fine-grained, single-repo, Contents-only token with short expiry | `docs/js/settings.js`: the token now defaults to **`sessionStorage`** (cleared when the tab/browser closes) and only moves to `localStorage` if the CEO explicitly opts in (`automb.rememberToken`); `migrateLegacyToken()` relocates a token written by the pre-fix build and deletes the stale `localStorage` copy. Set/forget/mask, no server round-trip. Guidance in `docs/README.md` | None | PARTIAL — **storage default fixed, enforcement still absent.** The durable-plaintext-at-rest exposure is materially reduced (default is no longer a token that survives browser restarts on a shared machine), but nothing in code inspects token shape, scope or expiry, so a classic repo-wide or org-wide PAT is still accepted silently. No test asserts the sessionStorage default, so the next edit can revert it undetected |
| 3.4.3 | Restrictive CSP on the dashboard origin | **Now present:** `docs/index.html` line 8 carries `<meta http-equiv="Content-Security-Policy">` with `default-src 'self'; connect-src 'self' https://api.github.com; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self'; img-src 'self' data:; base-uri 'none'; object-src 'none'; form-action 'none'`. GitHub Pages still cannot set response *headers*, so this is the meta-tag form | None | PARTIAL (was TBD) — a real CSP now exists and `connect-src` confines exfiltration to `api.github.com`, so the "single injected script silently ships the PAT anywhere" path is closed. Not `DONE`, for three reasons: (1) **no proving test** asserts the tag's presence or contents, so deleting it breaks nothing; (2) the meta form cannot carry `frame-ancestors` or HSTS, which are header-only; (3) `script-src 'self'` **permits any script committed into the repo** — see the `import()` row below, which is exactly that hole |
| 1.2.1, 1.3.2 | No string-built HTML; repo content rendered as DOM nodes | `docs/js/markdown.js` builds nodes via `createElement`/`textContent` for all fetched decision/memory content. Remaining `innerHTML` sites re-checked at current line numbers ([UX-3], app.js citation refreshed again by the branch-picker change — the previous `app.js:355`/`app.js:350` had drifted): `docs/app.js:422` and `docs/js/dashboard.js:93,190,237` assign static literals; `docs/js/icons.js:22` interpolates `shape`, selected from the frozen internal `SHAPES` map via `Object.hasOwn` with a fallback (the [UX-3] `info` square is a fifth static literal in that map), so no fetched or user data reaches it. The [UX-3] grouped/collapsible decisions view (`docs/js/decisions-view.js`) introduces zero `innerHTML` — DOM built via `createElement`/`textContent` throughout | None — no lint rule is wired for `docs/`, and per §2.2 the "ESLint frontend bans" gate does not exist for product code either | PARTIAL — still true by inspection, still not enforced. The next edit can regress it undetected, and the citation drift this entry corrects is that failure mode in miniature |
| 13.3.2 | Least privilege for the dashboard's write capability | App-level writes are confined to `decisions/*.md`: `docs/js/decisions.js` is the only caller of the write helpers in `docs/js/github-api.js` — since [DASH-1] that is `putFile`/`deleteFile` **plus** `createRef` (working branches) and `createPullRequest`, with read-only `getRef`/`listOpenPullRequests` alongside. `docs/README.md` §"Creating a fine-grained PAT" states plainly that GitHub's `Contents` permission is granted **for the whole repository**, that a `Contents: write` token can rewrite *any* file, and that the token now also needs **`Pull requests: write`** for the approve/reject/comment flow | None | PARTIAL — and **[DASH-1] widened the blast radius; recorded here in words, not buried**. The PAT still carries repo-wide Contents write, so anything executing on the dashboard origin can rewrite `SECURITY.md`, `.claude/hooks/guard.sh`, `package.json` or any other path — and with `Pull requests: write` added, the same token can now `POST /pulls` **and `PUT /pulls/<n>/merge`**. Before DASH-1, branch protection's require-PR rule was a real capability barrier against this token (its 409 on direct writes is the whole premise of `APPROVED-036`); after it, a token exfiltrated from the Pages origin can author **and merge** its own `decisions/*.md`-only PR — `frozen-effect --ci` exempts `decisions/` (since [BIND-2], all of it EXCEPT `decisions/BINDINGS.md`, which now needs a web-flow-verified commit or a covering ticket — so this stolen-token path can no longer quietly rewrite the binding ledger, though the `APPROVED-*` approval shape described here is unchanged), `gates` passes on a doc-only diff, and per the [SEC-8] row no human review is required on `main`. A self-merged `Scope: **` approval is therefore within the stolen-token capability envelope. The confinement remains convention, not capability; closing this still needs a server-side mediator holding the token, and the added scope raises what the mediator would be protecting |
| N/A (org governance; cf 8.2.1) | Dashboard-issued approvals gate the `packages/kernel` freeze | `docs/js/decisions.js` renames `PENDING-nnn` to `APPROVED-nnn` — since [DASH-1] (`decisions/APPROVED-036`) **on a dedicated working branch (`automb/<verb>-<nnn>-<slug>`) landed via a pull request the CEO merges**, never a direct commit to the configured branch (branch protection's `409 … must be made through a pull request` on `main` is what forced this). The sha re-check immediately before writing, the rollback of the created file if the delete leg fails, the H1 rewrite and `Decision: Option <X>` all survive on the branch, the PR opens only after both writes succeed (a half-applied rename structurally cannot reach a PR), and a new open-PR pre-check — run at action start AND re-run immediately before the PR opens — refuses a second transition while any `automb/{approve,reject,comment}-<nnn>-` PR for the same decision is open. That check **narrows, not closes**, the both-PRs-merge-cleanly hazard the cross-model review proved with real git (approve-PR + reject-PR both deleting PENDING from the shared base leaves `main` holding **both** an APPROVED and a REJECTED file, the approved one carrying live `Authorizes:`/`Scope:`): it is check-then-act over HTTP, and the review reproduced two sessions acting on one decision within the residual ~1-call window still opening both PRs — so the last line of defense is the CEO merging one PR and letting the other surface as refused/conflicted, stated here rather than advertised away (`APPROVED-012` shape). `.claude/hooks/guard.sh` `decision_approves_change()` no longer lifts the freeze on mere filename existence: a candidate counts only if its `Status:` line starts with `APPROVED` **and** it either contains `$CLAUDE_TICKET` or was touched on this branch vs `origin/main`; **exactly one** candidate lifts, zero or ≥2 fails closed | `scripts/test-hooks.sh` binding cases: ticket-bound lift allowed; different ticket blocked; two candidates ambiguous → blocked; `Status: PENDING` → blocked; branch-diff-bound lift allowed; two branch-committed decisions → blocked | PARTIAL (blanket-lift defect fixed) — the specific finding recorded here on 27/07/2026 (one filename permanently unfreezes every kernel path for every agent) **is closed and proven**. Still `PARTIAL` because the governance loop remains closed on itself: the same repo-wide PAT that the dashboard uses to *write* an `APPROVED-*kernel*.md` is what the hook then reads as authorization, and under the `CLAUDE_TICKET`-unset fallback any single decision file committed on the branch unfreezes every kernel path in it. The approver channel and the approved artifact share one credential |
| N/A (org governance; cf 8.2.1) | The CEO's approval channel produces an approval the guard will actually honour | **Fixed 28/07/2026** (`decisions/APPROVED-022` Option A, dashboard half). Previously `approveDecision` rewrote exactly three things — `Status:`, the H1, and an optional `Decision: Option X` — and the Approve button prompted for exactly one input, the option key. Neither ever wrote, prompted for, or validated `Authorizes:` or `Scope:`, while `decision_approves_change()` grants nothing without both. The documented approval channel therefore could not produce a live approval: `APPROVED-020` landed inert, `APPROVED-021` needed the CEO to hand-edit both lines in the GitHub UI (`74ca437`), and `APPROVED-022` is armed only through the markdown-blind parser bug. The flow now prompts for both fields, prefills `Authorizes:` from the header/`Blocks:` line and `Scope:` from the paths named in the chosen option, **refuses to write when either is empty** (explicitly *not* defaulting to `**`, which would turn every routine approval into a repo-wide unfreeze — strictly worse than an inert one, which at least fails closed), and inserts both into the decision's **header block** per `decisions/APPROVED-023`. **[DASH-1] (`decisions/APPROVED-036`) rebuilt the transport under this channel:** approve/reject/comment now write to a working branch and open a PR the CEO merges (the approval is live only when the PR lands), a success dialog names the PR so success is distinguishable from a no-op, the pre-action prompts describe the branch+PR reality, failure classes are distinct (a contents-write 409 means nothing landed anywhere; `PullRequestFailedError` means the branch holds the change and only the PR is missing, with a `Pull requests: write` hint on 403/404), and the APPROVED-022 empty-field guard still refuses **before any API call**. No `innerHTML` introduced — the new dialogs are `createElement`/`textContent` | Measured end-to-end 28/07/2026 against the **live** `decision_approves_change()` in a fixture repo, not asserted from the diff. A decision carrying what the new flow writes: in-`Scope` path → **GRANTED**; out-of-`Scope` `packages/kernel/**` → DENIED; a prefix ticket (`PENDING-99` against `PENDING-999`) → DENIED; an unrelated ticket → DENIED. **The same decision with the two lines stripped — exactly what the old flow wrote — → DENIED**, which is finding 3 reproduced and closed. Pure-function checks: empty `Authorizes:`/`Scope:`/whitespace-only/null all refuse before any API call; insertion is idempotent; the body below the header is byte-identical; and a decision whose *prose* argues against blanket scope by quoting `Scope: **` yields an **empty** prefill rather than a blanket one. **Second measurement, `APPROVED-024`, 28/07/2026:** approved through the *old* flow still live on `main` (Pages serves `docs/` from the default branch, so the fix above was not in play) and it **landed armed anyway**, because the filing agent had written `Authorizes:`/`Scope:` into the header block from `decisions/PENDING-000-template.md`. 13 probes against the live guard: the three named kernel tickets GRANTED on `packages/kernel/**`; three unnamed tickets DENIED on the same path; `DC-5` DENIED on `.claude/hooks/**`, `.claude/settings.json`, `package.json`, `.github/workflows/**`, `scripts/test-hooks.sh` and on the approval file itself; unset `$CLAUDE_TICKET` DENIED. So the grant arms exactly what it names and widens nothing. The template carrying the two fields is therefore an independent fix for the same defect, effective without the dashboard change — the first dashboard approval in this repo to land live with no hand-editing (020 inert, 021 hand-edited in `74ca437`, 022 armed only via the markdown-blind bug) | PARTIAL — the channel defect is closed and proven, and this row exists because none covered it before. Not `DONE` for three reasons, none of them cosmetic. (1) **Narrowed 29/07/2026 [GATE-1]** (`decisions/APPROVED-040` Option A) — the harness is now gate-run: `pnpm dashboard:probe` executes `scripts/probe-dashboard-approve.mjs` (89 assertions over the **stateful** GitHub fake that enforces real contents-API preconditions — the first probe of this flow whose double can refuse a write, after the review showed a canned-response mock certifying a broken retry) on every chain run, measured ~0.06s and fault-injection-proven in-chain, so an edit regressing the flow now reddens the push instead of waiting for a hand run. What stays open, unchanged by wiring: the probe's reach stops at the module boundary — `docs/js/decisions-view.js` (the success/confirmation dialogs, the refresh-failure separation) has no DOM harness and is proven by review inspection only, on all three review rounds. (2) The fields are still only as good as what the CEO types — the gate refuses empty, not wrong; a `Scope:` broader than the work needs is accepted silently. (3) The write still travels on the same repo-wide PAT the row above describes, now with `Pull requests: write` on top — the channel is hardened while its blast radius has grown |
| N/A (org guardrail; no ASVS 5.0 control governs agent hooks) | Hook guard actually blocks `git commit --no-verify` and plain force-push (AGENTS.md non-negotiable 2) | `.claude/hooks/guard.sh`, hardened under `decisions/APPROVED-012-make-guardrails-real.md` (supersedes SUPERSEDED-011) and **rearchitected under `decisions/APPROVED-013-guardrail-architecture.md`** (Option A, split the model by surface). Rules (b)/(c) — force-push, `--no-verify` — no longer tokenize argv: they are a **conservative deny over the raw command text**, so quotes, backslashes, `-C`/`-c`, `eval`, `xargs`, heredocs, absolute argv[0] and `bash -c` nesting stop mattering, at the documented cost of false positives on prose mentioning force-push. Rules (a)/(e) — Edit/Write freeze on `packages/kernel/` plus the protected-path list — keep `realpath -m` canonicalization and gained `.github/workflows/**`, `scripts/test-hooks.sh`, `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, `.claude/settings.local.json`, `decisions/APPROVED-*` and `decisions/SUPERSEDED-*`; `package.json` is content-aware (SEC-12). Authorization is anchored: `$CLAUDE_TICKET` must be set (unset always blocks; the branch-diff fallback is deleted) and named on a whole-token `Authorizes:` line of an `APPROVED` decision whose `Scope:` covers the path; exactly one candidate lifts. (1) It detects **after** the write, not before; the write is uncommitted when it fires and CI is the wall. (2) A command that writes a frozen path and reverts it inside one invocation is invisible to it (stated up front in APPROVED-021, not discovered later). (3) Deleting `.claude/hooks/` disables the in-container hook; only CI catches that, by the gate command failing outright. (4) **`--ci` mode does not police `decisions/` at all.** The dashboard commits CEO approvals onto the working branch, so every branch carrying a real approval adds a frozen `decisions/` file relative to its base — policing it in CI would redden exactly the approvals the system exists to honor, and nothing distinguishes a CEO commit from an agent commit because both travel on the same PAT. `decisions/` remains frozen in hook mode, which is where APPROVED-021 row 16 ("an agent authors its own `APPROVED-*.md`") actually lives, and an uncommitted one never authorizes. The residue is the credential-sharing loop the kernel-freeze-lift row below already carries as `PARTIAL`; closing it needs a separate approver credential, not a better hook. Established by a controlled A/B on throwaway PR #5: head `0ba3cdd` carried a failing `frozen-effect` check run and the PR reported `mergeable_state: blocked`; head `5a523c6` had `frozen-effect` green with only `gates` still pending and the same PR reported `unstable`. Since `unstable` is returned only when nothing *blocking* is outstanding, that second reading excludes a required-review explanation and confirms `gates` is not required, leaving the required check as the cause of the first. That reading was accurate when taken; `gates` was made required later the same day and re-measured on PR #7 (failing `gates`, green `frozen-effect` → `blocked`), so both CI jobs now block and no residue remains on this point. (6) **Closed 29/07/2026 [DC-46]** (`decisions/APPROVED-025` Option A) — was: authorization was per-BRANCH in `--ci` mode, so one correctly-scoped commit laundered every other in-`Scope` change on the branch (PR #5's unauthorized `.claude/hooks/PROBE-DELETE-ME.md` passed the `pull_request` run because the branch carried legitimate `[PENDING-021]` commits and APPROVED-021's `Scope:` covers `.claude/hooks/**`; only the `push`-context run caught it, and that run degenerates to the same whole-branch diff on a branch's first push, where `github.event.before` is all zeros). `--ci` now walks the range commit by commit: each non-merge commit's frozen changes must be covered by a ticket named in *that commit's own* message, merge commits and `decisions/` stay exempt (limitation (4) unchanged), and the first-push special case is gone because the verdict no longer depends on which base the fallback chain lands on. Moved off "open" on the strength of the named cases, not the code: `scripts/test-hooks.sh` `[effect --ci DC-46]` — the PR #5 laundering payload reproduced exactly (authorized `[TICKET-HOOKS]` commit + same-`Scope:` unauthorized commit in one push) exits 2 in both commit orders; the same payload through the first-push fallback (all-zero base SHA → `origin/main`) exits 2; an authorized-only branch through the same fallback exits 0; a multi-commit ticket naming its id in every frozen-touching commit exits 0. The stricter cost APPROVED-025 accepted is now live and stated: a legitimate multi-commit ticket must name its ticket id in **every** commit that touches a frozen path. **Correction, 29/07/2026 (post-merge review):** the first cut of this fix skipped merge commits entirely on the false premise that a merge carries nothing a non-merge commit didn't author — reproduced wrong: an evil merge writing `PWNED` into a frozen path passed `--ci` at exit 0 (the pre-DC-46 whole-branch check blocked it), a regression on the freeze's primary CI carrier. Closed by a **whole-range backstop** ([DC-46] hotfix): any frozen path changed across `MERGE_BASE..HEAD` that no non-merge commit in the range authored must be covered by `$CLAUDE_TICKET` or a ticket the merge commit itself names — merge-carried evil-merge and take-ours deltas now exit 2, while an innocent merge, a `merge main in`, and a merge that names its own covering ticket all still pass (both false-negative subclasses and the false-positive guards are in `scripts/test-hooks.sh` `[effect --ci DC-46]`). **The one residual this row stated — a frozen path changed by an authorized non-merge commit and further altered by a conflicting merge resolution judged on the commit, not the merge's re-alteration — is now CLOSED 02/08/2026 [GUARD-9]** (`decisions/APPROVED-072` Option A, re-audit 2026-08-02 §H1). The backstop's seen-path skip was unconditional (`[ -n "${CI_SEEN_FROZEN[$rel]:-}" ] && continue`), so a merge that rewrote a seen frozen path to unauthorized content at HEAD was judged by neither the per-commit walk (which saw only the non-merge commit's authorized content) nor the backstop (which skipped it) — MEASURED exit 0. The skip is now **blob-conditional**: `CI_SEEN_FROZEN` records the end-state blob each seen path was left at, and the backstop skips only when `HEAD:rel` still equals it; a merge re-alteration makes HEAD differ, so it falls through to the merge-carried judgment (require a merge-named covering ticket), fail-closed on any git error. `scripts/test-hooks.sh` `[effect --ci GUARD-9]`: the re-alteration blocks (exit 2, pre-fix 0), the same re-alteration under a covering merge ticket is granted, and a seen path the merge leaves untouched still skips cleanly (blob-match FP-guard) |

| N/A (org tooling; cf 1.2.1, 8.2.1) | The Settings **branch picker** cannot be used to steer the CEO onto an attacker-chosen branch, and its option labels are not a rendering channel | `docs/js/branch-select.js` (new) + `docs/app.js` Settings wiring + read-only `listBranches()` in `docs/js/github-api.js`. The branch control became a `<select>` populated from the repo's live branches (previously free text, which offered no dropdown at all on mobile). Three properties matter because this control sits on the **governance control path** — the picked branch is the ref every decision read AND every `APPROVED-*.md` write targets: (1) options are built with `createElement`/`textContent` only, never `innerHTML`, so a hostile branch name is inert text — verified in a real browser with `<img src=x onerror=…>` and `"><script>` names (no element children, `window.__XSS` never set); (2) **lookalike names are excluded from the pick list** — names carrying bidi/zero-width controls (`U+200B–200F`, `U+202A–202E`, `U+2066–2069`) or non-NFC forms are filtered out, so a pushed `main`+`U+200B` cannot sit visually identical to `main` and harvest a mis-click; any such name is reachable only by explicitly typing it through the "Other" escape hatch, and if it is the active branch it renders with its codepoints escaped and flagged; (3) no branch is synthesized on a live fetch, so the list cannot show a `main` the API says does not exist. The picked value reaches the API only through `encodeURIComponent` (`getFile`/`listDir`) or `JSON.stringify` (`putFile`/`deleteFile`), and reaches the UI only via `textContent` in the modal | `scripts/probe-branch-select.mjs` — 30 assertions: ordering/dedup/degrade, deleted-branch retention, `OTHER` resolution, the fake-`<select>` selection arms (modelling real DOM value-assignment, so a "selected" assertion is not tautological), and the lookalike/`\uXXXX`-label cases above. **In `pnpm gates` since 29/07/2026 [GATE-1]** (`decisions/APPROVED-040` Option A): `pnpm dashboard:probe` runs it on every chain run (measured ~0.05s; the step's pair totals ~0.1s) | PARTIAL — the spoofing and phantom-branch defects were **found in adversarial review before merge and are closed and proven**, but three residues are named rather than glossed. (2) Homoglyph confusables beyond bidi/zero-width (Cyrillic `а` vs Latin `a`) are **not** detected; a full confusables table is out of scope here and would need its own decision. An in-container agent cannot mint that signature (a local/pushed commit is never web-flow-signed; a contents-API PAT write is unsigned — measured on `a076ab2`, which also means the org dashboard's own decision-writer channel CANNOT grant here: grants go through the GitHub web editor). The MERGE shape is closed by reading the newest VERIFIED toucher under `--full-history` and requiring its blob to equal the read rev's, so a reachable newer verified revoke denies the resurrected older grant while the documented `git fetch` + merge pickup (whose blob equals the CEO grant it carries) still grants — both proven on the guard AND `--ci` surfaces, each mutation-verified to relaunder under the old `log -1`. **(ii-c, CLOSED in `--ci` by `decisions/APPROVED-062` Option A, [BIND-3]; hook mode stays best-effort, stated below.)** The merge fix enforced revocation only over commits REACHABLE from the read rev, and the read rev is an agent-controlled ref. An agent that makes the revoke UNREACHABLE — `git update-ref refs/heads/<b> <old-grant-sha>` (a guard-permitted command, measured exit 0) rewinding the branch past the revoke, or authoring a commit whose parent predates it — leaves the older CEO-signed grant as the newest reachable verified toucher and RESURRECTS it with `CLAUDE_TICKET` unset (measured end-to-end through the real pinned `guard.sh`: `git update-ref` → exit 0, then unbound ledger Edit → exit 0). So this channel authenticates ledger STATE (the CEO web-flow-signed this exact blob at some point) but NOT LATEST INTENT (this is the CEO's current grant): an agent still cannot mint an id the CEO never signed — the BLOCKER-1 property holds — but any id the CEO EVER signed stays resurrectable. This is a distinct vector from the `git read-tree -u`/`checkout-index` worktree-overwrite gap (the ledger-rev-STEERING vector `git update-ref`, not a worktree write); both are recorded in `memory/inbox/2026-07-31-bind2-followups.md`. The anchor now exists where it matters (`decisions/APPROVED-062` Option A, corrected in its own cross-model review round before merge): `frozen-effect-check.sh --ci` resolves `FROZEN_BINDINGS_BASE_ANCHOR` to a SERVER-AUTHORITATIVE ref — `origin/$GITHUB_BASE_REF`/`origin/HEAD`/`origin/main`/`origin/master` — resolved INDEPENDENTLY of the ref this run diffs against, and never the merge-base (a function of rewindable branch history). The independence is load-bearing and was the review BLOCKER on the first cut: on a push event `.github/workflows/ci.yml` passes `FROZEN_EFFECT_BASE_REF=github.event.before` (the branch's OWN previous tip, an agent-chosen commit) as the DIFF base, so anchoring to the diff base handed the agent the anchor and a rewound branch resurrected the revoked grant at exit 0 (measured on the resolvable-`FROZEN_EFFECT_BASE_REF` shape). Resolved from `origin/*` only, that hole is closed. `frozen_bindings_base_anchor_ok` (frozen lib) requires the anchor's newest VERIFIED ledger toucher to be an ancestor (`git merge-base --is-ancestor`) of EVERY rev the ledger is read as of: each judged commit, the merge backstop's HEAD, and the dirty-tree fold. In `--ci` the anchor is REQUIRED — if no `origin/*` ref resolves the ledger channel is denied outright (fail closed, never falling back to the agent-chosen diff base); the `$CLAUDE_TICKET`/commit-ticket channel does not pass through the anchor and is unaffected. The rewound branch and the commit-rooted-before-the-revoke shapes both get zero ledger ids in `--ci` — each measured resurrecting at exit 0 pre-fix and blocked post-fix in `scripts/test-hooks.sh`, on the PR shape, the first-push all-zero-base fallback, AND the resolvable-`FROZEN_EFFECT_BASE_REF` push shape — while the fetch+merge pickup flow and the honest up-to-date branch still grant. FRICTION, stated not hidden: the anchor is per-judged-rev, so any LATER CEO ledger action retroactively withholds ledger ids from a ledger-ONLY-authorized commit already on an in-flight branch — fail-closed, narrow (commit-ticket/`$CLAUDE_TICKET` auth is unaffected), and the remedy is to REBASE the branch onto the base ref, not merge it in (each commit is judged on its own ancestry; the remedy text and `decisions/BINDINGS.md` say so). Bounds, stated: HOOK mode is unchanged and best-effort BY CONSTRUCTION — every local ref is agent-owned, so the measured in-container resurrection still passes the hook alone (pinned as a suite case at exit 0) and the anchored `--ci`, a REQUIRED status check, is the enforcement point (the APPROVED-021 hook-vs-CI split); a grant the CEO signed only on a side branch and never landed on the base ref has no anchor to enforce its revocation against (the documented grant flow puts grants on the default branch, where the anchor holds); a branch behind the CEO's latest ledger action fails closed for ledger grants until it REBASES onto the base (merging does not clear an already-authored commit); and CI on an agent-owned FORK/clone anchors to that clone's agent-controlled `origin/main`, so a green push-CI run there is NOT the revocation wall — the base-repo pull-request required check (anchored to the base's `origin/main`) is. Note also the ledger read is now rev-scoped in `--ci` but the `decisions/APPROVED-*` enumeration still comes from the working tree (each commit judged against today's frozen decisions and its own ledger) — **ACCEPTED PERMANENTLY per `decisions/APPROVED-064` Option A [GUARD-2]**, documentation not debt: the asymmetry is tolerable precisely because decision files are frozen at BOTH `frozen-paths.lib.sh` enumeration sites (`is_protected_path` and `repo_has_protected_paths`), so exploiting it — adding or altering a covering decision mid-range — requires a write the same walk already flags as an offence elsewhere. The acceptance holds ONLY while decision files remain frozen at both sites; if either site ever unfreezes them, supersede APPROVED-064 and do its Option B (enumerate `decisions/APPROVED-*.md` as of each judged rev, mirroring the ledger's rev-scoping, with test-hooks cases both directions). The enumeration site in `decision_approves_change` carries a comment naming the asymmetry and the decision, so a future reader meets the ruling rather than rediscovering the finding. **(iii)** GitHub key rotation fails CLOSED (grants silently stop; re-pin is a scoped decision; `scripts/setup-env.sh` §3b surfaces it loudly) | `pnpm test:hooks` — 51 cases (see the §2.1 breakdown): all THREE BLOCKERs closed on BOTH enforcement surfaces (`guard.sh` AND `frozen-effect-check.sh`), each anchor case mutation-verified to go red without its fix (signature gate removed → 5 cases flip; rev-threading removed → the laundering case flips; `ci_exempt` carve-out reverted → the exemption case flips; CRLF trim removed → the CRLF case flips; validation removed → the `.*` case flips; `frozen_bindings_commit_verified` reverted to `git log -1 -- <path>` → both merge-resurrection cases relaunder), plus the real-pin `f4f4d58`/`d89e7a5` pair, plus the 12 [BIND-3] base-ref-anchor cases (`decisions/APPROVED-062`): the branch rewind, the commit-rooted-before-the-revoke, the first-push rewind, and the resolvable-`FROZEN_EFFECT_BASE_REF` push rewind (the cross-model-review BLOCKER) each measured resurrecting at exit 0 pre-fix and blocked exit 2 post-fix; the honest-branch, fetch+merge-pickup, first-push and honest-incremental-push controls still grant; the anchor-required-but-absent pair fails closed on ledger-only auth while the commit-ticket channel still passes; the retroactive-reddening friction case is pinned; and the hook-mode residual pinned honestly at exit 0 | PARTIAL — mechanism live, both review BLOCKERs closed and proven; the ledger ships EMPTY (grants nothing until the CEO commits a web-editor grant), and residues (i)/(ii) above keep this from DONE |

>    the unquoted form returns 2. `neutralize_and_split()` replaces

>    exist for the scan. This defeats **every channel the guard enumerates**,
>    not merely unlisted ones. That blanking was written to stop quoted flag
>    text from false-matching rules (b)/(c) — but APPROVED-013 moved (b)/(c)
>    to `flatten_raw`, so today it protects nothing and only destroys rule
>    (d)'s evidence. Beyond quoting: `/bin/rm`, `env` prefix, protected
>    *directories* (`rm -rf .claude/hooks`), `perl -i`, `python3 -c`,
>    `sort -o`, `rsync`, `tar -x`, `1>`/`1>>`/`>|`, `chmod`, `chown`,
>    `~` paths, `xargs`, `find -exec`, `cd` then a relative path, `$PWD`
>    expansion, brace expansion, and a symlink created earlier in the same
>    command (TOCTOU).
>    **Update 01/08/2026 [GUARD-4]** (`decisions/APPROVED-066` Option A):
>    the QUOTED-SPAN half of this class — the "one pair of quotes" that
>    headlines it — is CLOSED for rule (d): `neutralize_and_split` now
>    strips quotes and keeps content (single, double, dollar-single,
>    dollar-double, backslash escapes), so `cp /tmp/evil
>    ".claude/hooks/guard.sh"`, `git reset '--hard'` and `git
>    checkout-index '--a' -f` (the PR #116 measured reproducer) all exit 2,
>    proven both classes in `scripts/test-hooks.sh` [GUARD-4]. What stays
>    live from this row, stated not glossed: every NON-quoting spelling in
>    the list above (`perl -i`, `rsync`, `chmod`, TOCTOU symlinks, …), and
>    the EXPANSION class — `A=--a; git checkout-index $A -f`, `$(…)`,
>    backticks, brace expansion, and ANSI-C escapes inside dollar-single
>    quotes — which a text guard structurally cannot evaluate (it would
>    have to run the shell); `frozen-effect --ci` is the wall for the
>    modifying forms, and the clean-tree REVERT forms remain the narrow
>    stated residue. **PR #123 review update:** `git switch -f`/`--force`
>    (initially recorded here as a deferred residue) is now CLOSED — it is
>    the same `discard_changes` code path via git's `OPT__FORCE`, and
>    short-flag equivalents are in `APPROVED-066`'s frame (`clean
>    --force/-f`), so it was not a scope widening to close it; likewise
>    `git checkout --pathspec-from-file` and `git clean -i`/`--interactive`.
>    Residues that REMAIN measured-open OUT of the frame (adjacent, not
>    silently fixed and not silently omitted): (a) BROAD / ANCESTOR pathspecs —
>    **CLOSED 02/08/2026 by [GUARD-10]** (`decisions/APPROVED-077` Option A;
>    was TRACKED-OPEN here as `PENDING-077`). `git checkout -- .`,
>    `git restore .`, `git checkout -- ./`, `git checkout -- .claude`,
>    `git checkout -- .claude/hooks`, `git checkout HEAD -- .claude/hooks` and
>    `git restore .claude` each reverted frozen CHILDREN at measured exit 0
>    (`path_is_frozen` matches a frozen path and its descendants, never an
>    ANCESTOR), UNBACKED — a git revert leaves a CLEAN tree the effect wall
>    cannot see (the [GUARD-8] premise) — and inconsistent with the sibling
>    control (`git reset --hard`, the identical threat, was BLOCKED). The wall
>    is the wrapper-immune raw-text arm in `guard.sh`'s rule (e): whenever the
>    flattened command carries a `git` word, a checkout/restore/checkout-index
>    verb, and a token for which `path_is_frozen_ancestor` returns 0
>    (`frozen-paths.lib.sh` — ancestry over the SAME site-2 candidate list the
>    repo-wide verbs use, exposed as `frozen_protected_path_candidates` so the
>    two consumers cannot drift), it is denied `FROZEN_PATH=""` → `**`-gated,
>    exactly the `git reset --hard` posture and in the SAME rule as it.
>    **The opus cross-model review of the first cut measured a BLOCK** and is
>    recorded not glossed: the first cut placed the ancestor check ONLY in the
>    tokenizer, which the review defeated by effect — the no-`--` ref form
>    `git checkout HEAD .` (git resolves operand 1 as a tree-ish and the rest
>    as pathspecs, so the whole tree reverts with no `--`) and launchers the
>    cmd0 peel does not enumerate (`setarch`/`taskset`/`systemd-run git
>    checkout -- .`, each exit 0 pre-fix and effect-verified to restore a clean
>    tree). Moving the wall into rule (e) — which parses neither `--` nor the
>    launcher — closes both, the exact lesson GUARD-8 already paid for (the raw
>    scan is the wall, the tokenizer is the fast path). Discriminating, proven
>    both ways in `scripts/test-hooks.sh` [GUARD-10]: `git checkout -- docs`
>    (no frozen descendants), `git checkout main` (single operand, non-ancestor),
>    `git status .` (ancestor token but no revert verb), `find . | xargs grep -l
>    checkout` (no `git` word), and a literal in-scope revert under a path-grant
>    all stay allowed; a `**` grant lifts the ancestor deny, a path-scoped grant
>    does not (it cannot vouch for what it cannot enumerate). **Accepted
>    conservative FP, same class as the reset --hard raw arm's prose FP:** a
>    command carrying `git` + a revert verb + an ancestor token for UNRELATED
>    reasons over-blocks — `git checkout main; git add .`, and `git -C
>    <unrelated-repo> checkout -- .` (the raw arm is `-C`-blind, exactly like
>    the `-C <other> reset --hard` accepted FP) — recoverable by splitting the
>    commands or a `**` grant. **Disclosed residue, NOT claimed closed:** a
>    renamed git binary (no `git` token — the GUARD-8 renamed-binary residue);
>    an empty-string pathspec `git checkout -- ''` (the token vanishes on the
>    whitespace split, and modern git rejects it `rc 128` anyway); and a
>    `git checkout <ref> -- <dir>` that RE-CREATES a frozen path currently
>    ABSENT from the worktree (ancestry is anchored on on-disk existence, so a
>    candidate that does not exist cannot be an ancestor to sweep); (b) a global `-c
>    clean.requireForce=false` (or `--config`) override, which flips git's
>    default and lets `git -c clean.requireForce=false clean -d` delete at
>    exit 0 — it reaches the clean arm as an already-consumed git global
>    option, invisible to the sub-arg scan; closing it means teaching the
>    global-option walk config-value semantics, out of this convenience-net
>    ticket's frame; (c) a path-qualified interpreter — `/usr/bin/git reset
>    --hard` still exit 0, because every arm matches `cmd0` exactly (`case
>    "$cmd0" in git)`), the same pre-existing residue the `git push --force`
>    line carries (an unclaimed win: quote/backslash stripping now
>    normalizes `\git` to `git`, so `\git reset --hard` went exit 0 → 2);
>    (d) redirect spellings `>|`/`1>`/`&>` bypass the `>`/`>>` scan (`1>` is
>    already listed above; `&>` is added to that enumeration here). Each is
>    a text-guard gap with `frozen-effect --ci` as the wall for its
>    modifying forms.
>    **Update 01/08/2026 [GUARD-5]** (`decisions/APPROVED-067` Option A):
>    the `perl -i` entry in the stays-live list above — and its siblings
>    `ruby -i` and `awk`/`gawk` `-i inplace`/`inplace=1` — is CLOSED for
>    the ergonomic, token-named forms: those interpreters name their
>    in-place target as a bare positional token exactly like `sed -i`, so
>    they now go through the same token scan (all four probe cases pinned
>    BLOCKED in `scripts/test-hooks.sh` with flag-gated and path-gated
>    controls; each was exit 0 pre-fix while the `sed -i` control blocked).
>    **Claimed exactly, per APPROVED-067's token-scanner limit:** an inline
>    PROGRAM that computes its target — `python3 -c
>    'open("packages/"+"kernel/x","w")'`, `perl -e`, `ruby -e`, `node -e`
>    — never names the path as an argv token, and NO token scanner can
>    close that class without parsing arbitrary interpreter code. It is
>    **accepted residue, deliberately not claimed closed** (Option B —
>    blocking inline-program interpreters wholesale — was rejected for its
>    daily false-positive tax); the `python3 -c` control in the suite pins
>    it as residue rather than letting an over-broad arm land unnoticed,
>    and `frozen-effect --ci` remains the wall for the modifying forms.
>    `python3 -c open(w)` accordingly moves from the stays-live list to
>    stated-residue-by-design; the remaining non-quoting spellings there
>    (`rsync`, `chmod`, TOCTOU symlinks, …) are unchanged.
>
>    **Update 02/08/2026 [GUARD-8]** (`decisions/APPROVED-071` Option A,
>    the CRITICAL of the 2026-08-02 re-audit): APPROVED-067's "CLOSED at
>    PreToolUse" claim above was **falsified for the WRAPPED forms** and is
>    corrected here. The perl/ruby/awk `-i` arms (and every verb arm) matched
>    an EXACT first token, so a launcher prefix (`env`/`timeout`/`command`/
>    `exec`/`nice`/`sudo`/`stdbuf`/`nohup`/`setsid`/`xargs`) or a
>    path-qualified binary (`/usr/bin/perl`, `/bin/sed`) evaded the token scan
>    entirely — `env perl -i <frozen>` was exit 0 while the bare form blocked
>    (measured). So pre-GUARD-8 the wrapped interpreter-in-place writes were
>    **effect-wall-backed residue, NOT PreToolUse-closed**. GUARD-8's cmd0
>    NORMALIZER (basename + iterative launcher peel) now dispatches the verb
>    arms on the real command, so the launcher/path-qualified wrapped forms of
>    ALL these arms ARE closed at PreToolUse again (pinned in the `[GUARD-8]`
>    suite section). What stays effect-wall-backed residue, unchanged: the
>    inline-program computed-path class above (`perl -e`/`python3 -c`
>    constructing the path), which no token scanner or launcher-peel can reach.
>    Separately, the no-backstop git-REVERT class — which leaves a CLEAN tree
>    the effect wall canNOT see — is additionally routed to the quote/wrapper/
>    path/indirection-immune raw-text scan (rule (e)), the only wall for a
>    trace-erasing wrapped revert; its cost is the documented conservative FPs
>    (prose literally spelling `git … reset --hard`, and `-C <other repo>`
>    reverts on a protected host), the same accepted-FP class rules (b)/(c)
>    carry, pinned as `[false-positive GUARD-8]` controls. **Cross-model
>    review, four rounds (opus), same PR:** the PATH-ATTRIBUTABLE reverts
>    (`checkout -- <path>`/`restore`/named `checkout-index`, also clean-tree,
>    no-backstop) needed their own closure — round 1 added a per-path raw scan
>    for wrappers that KEEP THE PATH IN TEXT; rounds 2-3 chased the OUT-OF-BAND
>    forms (find `{}`/xargs stdin, GNU `parallel`, `while read`/`for`, `$(…)`/
>    `$var` pathspecs) through a frame-name list and then a raw-text adjacency
>    regex — each defeated one spelling further out (`./` prefix, dropped or
>    inserted `--`, a `do git checkout …` loop body). Round 4 fixed the CLASS
>    at the TOKENIZER: `grd8_operand_unattributable` flags any PARSED pathspec
>    operand carrying a shell expansion (`$VAR`/`$(…)`/backtick) or the `find`
>    `{}` placeholder in the checkout/restore/checkout-index arms (plus a
>    `do`/`then`/`else` body-keyword peel), position-aware so prefix/moved-`--`
>    cannot dodge it; the one surviving raw-text arm is the `xargs`/`parallel`
>    stdin frame, whose operand never becomes a token. **Round 5** found that
>    operand predicate was a metachar DENYLIST (`$`/backtick/`{}`) that missed
>    pathname GLOBBING — a wildcard in the DIRECTORY component
>    (`git checkout -- .claude/*/guard.sh`, `.claude/hook?/guard.sh`,
>    `.claude/[h]ooks/guard.sh`, and the `restore`/`checkout-index` forms) hides
>    the literal `.claude/hooks` prefix from `path_is_frozen` while bash expands
>    the pattern to the frozen file at runtime (exit 0, measured). Fixed by
>    FLIPPING the predicate to a literal-path ALLOWLIST — attributable iff the
>    operand is a plain literal in `[A-Za-z0-9._/@+-]`, else conservative-deny
>    — which closes `$`-expansion, `*`/`?`/`[…]`, brace lists and tilde in one
>    predicate instead of enumerating dangerous chars. **Round 5b** dropped `:`
>    from that charset: `:` is git's PATHSPEC-MAGIC sigil (`:/path` repo-top-
>    relative, `:!`/`:^`/`:(…)`), so `git checkout -- :/.claude/hooks/guard.sh`
>    used only otherwise-safe chars yet reverted the frozen file at runtime while
>    `path_is_frozen` (which does not model git's pathspec grammar) missed the
>    literal `:/…` (exit 0, measured) — dropping `:` denies the whole magic-
>    pathspec class in one char, and no frozen path contains `:`. `@` is kept
>    (git's `@{…}` rev-magic is already denied by the brace; a plain `@` is a
>    literal filename). **Stated residue,
>    disclosed not claimed closed:** `git checkout <expansion>` WITHOUT `--`
>    stays exit 0 (branch-vs-pathspec ambiguity — closing it would over-block
>    `git checkout $BRANCH`; `restore`/`checkout-index`, which are unambiguous,
>    ARE closed), alongside the renamed-binary and runtime-verb-construction
>    floor — each pinned by a `[GUARD-8 review4 residue]` / `[review3 residue]`
>    control so it cannot be mistaken for coverage. Accepted fail-safe FP: a
>    glob revert of NON-frozen files (`git checkout -- src/*.ts`) is `**`-gated
>    over-blocked (a text guard cannot know statically what a glob expands to).
> 2. **The conservative deny is itself evadable.** Rules (b)/(c) match raw
>    substrings, so any spelling that splits or hides them passes:
>    `git -c alias.f='push --force' f origin main`,
>    `git -c alias.ci='commit --no-verify' ci -m wip`,
>    `{git,push,--force,origin,main}` (brace expansion),
>    `F=--forc; git push ${F}e origin main` (variable concatenation),
>    `git-push --force` (dashed plumbing spelling), and
>    `git commit --no-veri` (git accepts unique long-option abbreviations).
>    Rule (b) also never models `git push origin --delete main`,
>    `git push --mirror`, or `--force-with-lease origin HEAD`.
> 3. **An agent can author its own authorization.** `is_protected_path` has
>    no bare-directory case, so `cp /tmp/APPROVED-999-pwn.md decisions/`
>    returns exit 0 — an agent writes its own `APPROVED-*.md`, which is
>    precisely what lifts the kernel and hook freeze. `rm -f
>    decisions/APPROVED*` is likewise unmatched, because globs are never
>    expanded. This is the governance root of trust, not a parsing detail.
>
> **Disposition.** The guardrail is **not** a control an auditor should be
> shown, and no row above claims `DONE`. The proving test cited for the guard
> row passes 239/239 while the rule it is cited for is disarmed by a quotation
> mark — the one-sided-suite failure `.claude/agent-memory/reviewer/rubrics/
> fake-done.md` §1 exists to catch. Reviewers should note this is the **fourth
> round** of the same pattern: APPROVED-010, APPROVED-012 and APPROVED-013 each
> closed every payload named and shipped a larger green suite that did not
> cover the next evasion. `PENDING-021` therefore recommends abandoning text
> inspection for effect verification rather than patching the 27.

---

## 13. Certification track (context for auditors)

- **Bar:** OWASP ASVS 5.0 **Level 2**; auditors test per OWASP WSTG.
- **Auditor requirement — RESOLVED (CEO, 29/07/2026): STQC specifically is
  required.** The open verification that stood here since this section was
  written ("confirm whether STQC specifically or any CERT-In empanelled
  auditor is acceptable") is answered by the certificate-consuming
  authority's requirement. Planning consequences, recorded so scheduling
  starts from reality: STQC lab engagement carries government-lab lead
  times and queue-based scheduling (book early, well before the internal
  pre-audit completes), the fee schedule is STQC's own (no competitive
  quotes), and the change-classification matrix below must be agreed **in
  writing with STQC** — its acceptance of the delta-audit model is now the
  single most schedule-critical unknown in the certification track.
- **Sequence:** internal pre-audit (own pen test + green §2 gates + this
  matrix complete for shipped scope) → auditor engagement with the
  change-classification matrix agreed **in writing** → fix/retest →
  clearance certificate → certified release train + annual re-audit.
- **Change classification (summary; full text archived):** cosmetic/UI →
  self-attest · new read-only screen on audited APIs → delta scan · new
  module → delta audit · kernel/authz/session/crypto → full re-audit.
- Certificates are **version-bound, ~1 year max** — the kernel boundary
  exists to make every re-audit a delta, not an excavation.

Two things were wrong with that. First, PR #5 produced **two** runs on one head SHA — a `push` run (base `github.event.before`) that failed, and a `pull_request` run (base `main`) that **passed** — and the failing log quoted in SEC-3e was the push run's. Second, the `blocked` reading was taken while checks were still pending, so it showed only that *something* was required, and SEC-3e correctly flagged but could not exclude a required review. Re-measured properly as a controlled A/B on the same PR: head `0ba3cdd` (failing `frozen-effect`) → `blocked`; head `5a523c6` (`frozen-effect` green, only `gates` pending) → **`unstable`**. `unstable` is returned only when nothing blocking is outstanding, which excludes a required review, confirms `gates` is not required, and leaves the required check as the cause of the first reading. **`frozen-effect` is required; the SEC-3e conclusion stands on sound evidence now.** Two further facts fall out. (a) **No pull-request review is required on `main`** — an unreviewed PR is mergeable once the required check is green, so DESIGN.md §9's "only reviewed merges touch main" holds only in the weak sense that direct pushes are refused; the review half is convention, and this is the second time a §9 claim has proved stronger on paper than in the forge (cf `064b2a9`). (b) **`--ci` authorization is per-branch, not per-commit** — the probe's unauthorized `.claude/hooks/**` file passed the `pull_request` run because the branch carried legitimate `[PENDING-021]` commits and APPROVED-021's `Scope:` covers that prefix. One correctly-scoped commit launders every other in-`Scope` change on the branch; only the `push`-context run, diffing against the previous branch head, caught it. | 30/07/2026 | [MEM-2] **`decisions/APPROVED-045` implemented (Option A) — the decision ledger's completeness is mechanically checked, and the deadline staleness false positive is fixed.** Closes audit **H-4**'s mechanical half and **N-12**. **The gap was five times wider than H-4 says:** re-measured at MEM-2 start, **eleven** decisions had no row of any kind — `009`, `010`, `011` (SUPERSEDED), `012` and `045`–`051`. H-4 named 042/043 and, being scoped to what the scribe had recently skipped, never swept the whole directory — the same instance-not-class shape as H-1/H-5. All eleven rows plus `PENDING-052` landed; `APPROVED-045`'s `Scope:` covers `memory/decisions.md` precisely so this repair could be made, since without the rows the new check would redden the chain on day one. `scripts/memory-check.sh` now requires a row for every `APPROVED-*`/`SUPERSEDED-*`, matched by decision **number** under any status prefix so a row written before approval renamed the file still counts (the same number-not-filename rule `tickets-check.sh` uses for `gate_decision`). A **grace window** (`LEDGER_GRACE_DAYS`, default 3, clocked from the decision file's first commit date) keeps it report-only immediately after approval and structural past that — a check demanding a row instantly would make the approval commit itself un-mergeable, the defect `tickets-check.sh`'s `gate_decision` rule deliberately avoids. It **fails open** when git cannot date the file (no repo, shallow clone, uncommitted decision), reporting rather than blocking, because this is the one structural check whose input is external to the tree; every such case is reported, never silent. **N-12, and the first fix was wrong in a way the probe caught:** recording a decision's `Deadline:` in a rollup tripped the staleness heuristic, which greps every ISO date and cannot tell *a date a note is about* from *a date it was written* (`STALE: state.md — Updated: 2026-07-29 is older than a date found elsewhere in the tree (2026-08-14)`). Draft 1 kept only dates on an `as_of:`/`Updated:` field — which killed the false positive **and a true one**, because `memory/decisions.md` and `lessons.md` are tables whose first column *is* the authored-at date with no label, so the existing "summary older than the notes it covers" case went quiet. Narrowing a heuristic until the false positive disappears takes true positives with it. Each arm now matches its whole ladder; measured ambiguity boundaries bound them (`switch --d` is git-rejected ambiguous vs `--detach` → the ladder starts at `--di`; `--pathspec-f` ambiguous vs `--pathspec-file-nul` → the restore ladder stops at `--pathspec-fr`), and per-arm false-positive controls pin that no ladder over-matches a legitimate neighbor. `git restore --pathspec-from-file=<f>` (reads restored paths OFF argv — the `checkout-index --stdin` class, measured exit 0) is conservative-denied in both spellings; `stash` needs no ladder (git does not abbreviate stash SUBCOMMANDS — `git stash po` → exit 128, measured) and `read-tree` needs none (`-u` short-only) — both stated in `guard.sh` rather than silently skipped. (2) **Quoted-token bypass closed** (§12A class 1 — the auditor-note row-1 headline): `neutralize_and_split` now strips quotes and keeps content (single, double, dollar-single, dollar-double, backslash escapes; in-quote whitespace carried as an STX 0x02 marker so a quoted multi-word argument stays one shell word, converted back before path tests), so `git reset '--hard'`, `git checkout-index '--a' -f`, quoted frozen paths, and `--ha\rd` all exit 2; the stale comment justifying quote-blanking as protecting rules (b)/(c) (expired when `APPROVED-013` moved those to `flatten_raw`) is corrected in place. **Class 2 stays open by construction and is stated, not patched:** variable/command expansion and ANSI-C escapes cannot be closed by a text guard; `frozen-effect --ci` is the wall for modifying writes, the clean-tree revert forms remain the narrow residue. **Adjacent measured residues recorded, not silently fixed** (out of `APPROVED-066`'s frame): `git restore .`/`:/` broad pathspecs and the class-2 expansion residue — each still exit 0, each owed its own disposition. **PR #123 cross-model review (opus) follow-ups, same PR:** the review measured three same-class channels the first cut left live and they were closed rather than deferred — `git clean -i`/`--interactive` (interactive mode deletes without `-f`; the arm's comment had FALSELY asserted "none deletes without -f/--force" — corrected, and a `-c clean.requireForce=false` override recorded as a NEW stated residue), `git checkout --pathspec-from-file` (the off-argv channel closed on `restore` but left open on `checkout` in the first cut), and `git switch -f`/`--force` (git's `OPT__FORCE` sets `discard_changes` — a shorter spelling bypassing the whole ladder; short-flag equivalents are in `APPROVED-066`'s frame per the sibling `clean --force/-f` scope, so closing it was not a widening); plus a `-C`-with-space round-trip case and two documented-conservative quoted-value false positives (`clean -e '-f'`, `switch -c '--discard-changes'` over-BLOCK, failing safe). §2.1 count 551 → **612** (61 new cases, both fault classes per arm; +22 from the review follow-ups; re-measured by running the suite). `org/hooks-contract.md` + `docs/MIGRATION.md` reconciled: the abbreviation-ladder requirement now applies to EVERY long-option git arm a porting target reimplements, not just `checkout-index`. **One rule-9 note left as follow-up:** `org/manifest.json`'s prose enumeration of the repo-wide git verbs does not name `restore`/`checkout --pathspec-from-file`; Three `scripts/test-hooks.sh` `[effect --ci GUARD-9]` cases, mutation-proven red-first (reverting to the boolean skip flips only the exit-2 case to 0): the re-alteration blocks (exit 2), the same re-alteration under a covering merge ticket is granted (exit 0 — proving blob-equality does not blanket-deny), and a seen path the merge leaves at its authorized end-state still skips cleanly (blob-match FP-guard). An API that serializes `err.message` for anything it does not recognise ships the query structure to the client.
